href="/wiki/Eug%C3%A8ne-Gabriel-Gervais-Laurent_Tisserant" class="mw-redirect" title="Eugène-Gabriel-Gervais-Laurent Tisserant">Eugène-Gabriel-Gervais-Laurent Tisserant</a></th><td class="infobox-data">25 July 1937</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Francesco Benedetto Cialeo</th><td class="infobox-data">29 October 1939</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Carlo_Confalonieri" title="Carlo Confalonieri">Carlo Confalonieri</a></th><td class="infobox-data">4 May 1941</td></tr></tbody></table></td></tr></tbody></table> 
<p><b>Pope Pius XII</b> (<a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Pio XII</i>), born <b>Eugenio Maria Giuseppe Giovanni Pacelli</b> (<small>Italian pronunciation:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="it-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Italian" title="Help:IPA/Italian">[euˈdʒɛːnjo maˈriːa dʒuˈzɛppe dʒoˈvanni paˈtʃɛlli]</a></span>; 2 March 1876&#160;&#8211;&#32;9 October 1958), was head of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> and sovereign of the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City State</a> from 2 March 1939 until his death in October 1958. Before <a href="/wiki/1939_papal_conclave" title="1939 papal conclave">his election to the papacy</a>, he served as secretary of the <a href="/wiki/Section_for_Relations_with_States_(Roman_Curia)" class="mw-redirect" title="Section for Relations with States (Roman Curia)">Department of Extraordinary Ecclesiastical Affairs</a>, <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Nuncio_to_Germany" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Nuncio to Germany">papal nuncio to Germany</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a>, in which capacity he worked to conclude treaties with European and Latin American nations, including the <i><a href="/wiki/Reichskonkordat" title="Reichskonkordat">Reichskonkordat</a></i> with the <a href="/wiki/German_Reich" title="German Reich">German Reich</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While the Vatican was officially neutral during <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, the <i>Reichskonkordat</i> and his leadership of the Catholic Church during the war remain the subject of controversy — including allegations of public silence and inaction about the fate of the Jews.<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> Pius employed diplomacy to aid the victims of the Nazis during the war and, through directing the church to provide discreet aid to Jews and others, saved hundreds of thousands of lives.<sup id="cite_ref-EB_Pius_XII_Assessment_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EB_Pius_XII_Assessment-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-L&#39;oro_di_Pio_XII_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-L&#39;oro_di_Pio_XII-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Pius maintained <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII_and_the_German_Resistance" title="Pope Pius XII and the German Resistance">links to the German Resistance</a>, and shared intelligence with the Allies. His strongest public condemnation of genocide was considered inadequate by the Allied Powers, while the Nazis viewed him as an Allied sympathizer who had dishonoured his policy of Vatican neutrality.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During his papacy, the Catholic Church issued the <a href="/wiki/Decree_against_Communism" title="Decree against Communism">Decree against Communism</a>, declaring that Catholics who profess <a href="/wiki/Communist" class="mw-redirect" title="Communist">communist</a> doctrine are to be <a href="/wiki/Excommunicated" class="mw-redirect" title="Excommunicated">excommunicated</a> as <a href="/wiki/Apostates" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostates">apostates</a> from the Christian faith. <a href="/wiki/Persecutions_of_the_Catholic_Church_and_Pius_XII" title="Persecutions of the Catholic Church and Pius XII">The church experienced severe persecution</a> and mass deportations of Catholic clergy in the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Bloc" title="Eastern Bloc">Eastern Bloc</a>. He explicitly invoked <i><a href="/wiki/Ex_cathedra" class="mw-redirect" title="Ex cathedra">ex cathedra</a></i> <a href="/wiki/Papal_infallibility" title="Papal infallibility">papal infallibility</a> with the <a href="/wiki/Dogma_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Dogma in the Catholic Church">dogma</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Assumption_of_Mary" title="Assumption of Mary">Assumption of Mary</a> in his <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_constitution" title="Apostolic constitution">Apostolic constitution</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Munificentissimus_Deus" title="Munificentissimus Deus">Munificentissimus Deus</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> His <a href="/wiki/List_of_encyclicals_of_Pope_Pius_XII" title="List of encyclicals of Pope Pius XII">forty-one encyclicals</a> include <i><a href="/wiki/Mystici_Corporis_Christi" title="Mystici Corporis Christi">Mystici Corporis Christi</a></i>, on the Church as the <a href="/wiki/Mystical_Body_of_Christ" class="mw-redirect" title="Mystical Body of Christ">Mystical Body of Christ</a>; <i><a href="/wiki/Mediator_Dei" title="Mediator Dei">Mediator Dei</a></i> on liturgy reform; and <i><a href="/wiki/Humani_generis" title="Humani generis">Humani generis</a></i>, in which he instructed <a href="/wiki/Theology" title="Theology">theologians</a> to adhere to episcopal teaching and allowed that the human body might have <a href="/wiki/Evolution" title="Evolution">evolved</a> from earlier forms. He eliminated the Italian majority in the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a> in 1946.
</p><p>After he died in 1958, Pope Pius XII was succeeded by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">John XXIII</a>. In the process toward sainthood, his cause for canonization was opened on 18 November 1965 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Paul VI</a> during the final session of the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a>. He was made a <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">John Paul II</a> in 1990 and <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Benedict XVI</a> declared Pius XII <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a> on 19 December 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-Pitel_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pitel-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r886046785">.mw-parser-output .toclimit-2 .toclevel-1 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-3 .toclevel-2 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-4 .toclevel-3 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-5 .toclevel-4 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-6 .toclevel-5 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-7 .toclevel-6 ul{display:none}</style><div class="toclimit-3"><meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" /></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Early_life_of_Pope_Pius_XII" title="Early life of Pope Pius XII">Early life of Pope Pius XII</a></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Eugenio1882.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Eugenio1882.jpg/150px-Eugenio1882.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="332" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Eugenio1882.jpg/225px-Eugenio1882.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Eugenio1882.jpg/300px-Eugenio1882.jpg 2x" data-file-width="630" data-file-height="1396" /></a><figcaption>Eugenio Pacelli at the age of six in 1882</figcaption></figure>
<p>Eugenio Maria Giuseppe Giovanni Pacelli was born on the second day of Lent, 2 March 1876, in <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a> into a family of intense Catholic piety with a history of ties to the papacy (the "<a href="/wiki/Black_Nobility" class="mw-redirect" title="Black Nobility">Black Nobility</a>"). His parents were <a href="/w/index.php?title=Filippo_Pacelli&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Filippo Pacelli (page does not exist)">Filippo Pacelli</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://it.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filippo_Pacelli" class="extiw" title="it:Filippo Pacelli">it</a>&#93;</span> (1837–1916) and Virginia (née Graziosi) Pacelli (1844–1920). His grandfather <a href="/w/index.php?title=Marcantonio_Pacelli&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Marcantonio Pacelli (page does not exist)">Marcantonio Pacelli</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://it.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcantonio_Pacelli" class="extiw" title="it:Marcantonio Pacelli">it</a>&#93;</span> had been Under-Secretary in the Papal Ministry of Finances<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> and then Secretary of the Interior under <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX" title="Pope Pius IX">Pope Pius IX</a> from 1851 to 1870 and helped found the Vatican's newspaper, <i><a href="/wiki/L%27Osservatore_Romano" title="L&#39;Osservatore Romano">L'Osservatore Romano</a></i> in 1861.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> His cousin, <a href="/wiki/Ernesto_Pacelli" title="Ernesto Pacelli">Ernesto Pacelli</a>, was a key financial advisor to <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a>; his father, Filippo Pacelli, a <a href="/wiki/Franciscan" class="mw-redirect" title="Franciscan">Franciscan</a> <a href="/wiki/Third_order" title="Third order">tertiary</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> was the dean of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Rota" title="Roman Rota">Roman Rota</a>; and his brother, <a href="/wiki/Francesco_Pacelli" title="Francesco Pacelli">Francesco Pacelli</a>, became a <a href="/wiki/Laity" title="Laity">lay</a> <a href="/wiki/Canon_law" title="Canon law">canon lawyer</a> and the legal advisor to <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a>, in which role he negotiated the <a href="/wiki/Lateran_Treaty" title="Lateran Treaty">Lateran Treaty</a> in 1929 with <a href="/wiki/Benito_Mussolini" title="Benito Mussolini">Benito Mussolini</a>, bringing an end to the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Question" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Question">Roman Question</a>.
</p><p>Together with his brother Francesco (1872–1935) and his two sisters, Giuseppina (1874–1955) and Elisabetta (1880–1970),<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> he grew up in the <a href="/wiki/Parione" title="Parione">Parione</a> district in the centre of Rome. Soon after the family had moved to Via Vetrina in 1880, he began school at the convent of the French <a href="/wiki/Sisters_of_Divine_Providence" class="mw-redirect" title="Sisters of Divine Providence">Sisters of Divine Providence</a> in the Piazza Fiammetta. The family worshipped at <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_in_Vallicella" title="Santa Maria in Vallicella">Chiesa Nuova</a>. Eugenio and the other children made their <a href="/wiki/First_Communion" title="First Communion">First Communion</a> at this church and Eugenio served there as an <a href="/wiki/Altar_boy" class="mw-redirect" title="Altar boy">altar boy</a> from 1886. In 1886 too he was sent to the private school of Professor Giuseppe Marchi, close to the <a href="/wiki/Piazza_Venezia" title="Piazza Venezia">Piazza Venezia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> In 1891 Pacelli's father sent Eugenio to the Liceo Ennio Quirino Visconti Institute, a state school situated in what had been the <a href="/wiki/Collegio_Romano" class="mw-redirect" title="Collegio Romano">Collegio Romano</a>, the premier Jesuit university in Rome.
</p><p>In 1894, aged 18, Pacelli began his theology studies at Rome's oldest seminary, the <a href="/wiki/Almo_Collegio_Capranica" title="Almo Collegio Capranica">Almo Collegio Capranica</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> and in November of the same year, registered to take a philosophy course at the Jesuit <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Gregorian_University" title="Pontifical Gregorian University">Pontifical Gregorian University</a> and theology at the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Roman_Athenaeum_S._Apollinare" class="mw-redirect" title="Pontifical Roman Athenaeum S. Apollinare">Pontifical Roman Athenaeum S. Apollinare</a>. He was also enrolled at the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Rome_La_Sapienza" class="mw-redirect" title="University of Rome La Sapienza">State University, La Sapienza</a> where he studied modern languages and history. At the end of the first academic year however, in the summer of 1895, he dropped out of both the Capranica and the Gregorian University. According to his sister Elisabetta, the food at the Capranica was to blame.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Having received a special dispensation he continued his studies from home and so spent most of his seminary years as an external student. In 1899 he completed his education in Sacred Theology with a doctoral degree awarded on the basis of a short dissertation and an oral examination in <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Church_career">Church career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Church career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Priest_and_monsignor">Priest and monsignor</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Priest and monsignor">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pacelliordenado.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Pacelliordenado.jpg/150px-Pacelliordenado.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="198" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Pacelliordenado.jpg/225px-Pacelliordenado.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Pacelliordenado.jpg/300px-Pacelliordenado.jpg 2x" data-file-width="350" data-file-height="463" /></a><figcaption>Pacelli on the day of his ordination: 2 April 1899</figcaption></figure>
<p>While all other candidates from the Rome diocese were ordained in the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Saint_John_Lateran" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilica of Saint John Lateran">Basilica of Saint John Lateran</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Gerard_Noel,_p.9_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gerard_Noel,_p.9-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Pacelli was <a href="/wiki/Holy_Orders" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Orders">ordained</a> a priest on <a href="/wiki/Easter_Sunday" class="mw-redirect" title="Easter Sunday">Easter Sunday</a>, 2 April 1899 alone in the private chapel of a family friend the <a href="/wiki/Vicegerent" title="Vicegerent">Vicegerent</a> of Rome, <a href="/wiki/Francesco_di_Paola_Cassetta" title="Francesco di Paola Cassetta">Francesco di Paola Cassetta</a>. Shortly after ordination he began postgraduate studies in canon law at Sant'Apollinaire. He received his first assignment as a <a href="/wiki/Curate" title="Curate">curate</a> at <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_in_Vallicella" title="Santa Maria in Vallicella">Chiesa Nuova</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-marchione193_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marchione193-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> In 1901, he entered the <a href="/wiki/Section_for_Relations_with_States_(Roman_Curia)" class="mw-redirect" title="Section for Relations with States (Roman Curia)">Congregation for Extraordinary Ecclesiastical Affairs</a>, a sub-office of the <a href="/wiki/Secretariat_of_State_(Vatican)" class="mw-redirect" title="Secretariat of State (Vatican)">Vatican Secretariat of State</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Pietro_Gasparri" title="Pietro Gasparri">Pietro Gasparri</a>, the recently appointed undersecretary at the Department of Extraordinary Affairs, had underscored his proposal to Pacelli to work in the "Vatican's equivalent of the Foreign office" by highlighting the "necessity of defending the Church from the onslaughts of secularism and liberalism throughout Europe".<sup id="cite_ref-Gerard_Noel,_p._10_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gerard_Noel,_p._10-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Pacelli became an <i>apprendista</i>, an apprentice, in Gasparri's department. In January 1901 he was also chosen, by Pope Leo XIII himself, according to an official account, to deliver condolences on behalf of the Vatican to King <a href="/wiki/Edward_VII" title="Edward VII">Edward VII</a> of the UK after the death of <a href="/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria">Queen Victoria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-marchione9_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marchione9-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:MarrydelValpacelli.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/MarrydelValpacelli.jpg/150px-MarrydelValpacelli.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="207" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/MarrydelValpacelli.jpg/225px-MarrydelValpacelli.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/MarrydelValpacelli.jpg/300px-MarrydelValpacelli.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1090" data-file-height="1507" /></a><figcaption>The Serbian Concordat, 24 June 1914. Present for the Vatican were <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Merry_del_Val" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal Merry del Val">Cardinal Merry del Val</a> and next to him, Pacelli.</figcaption></figure>
<p>By 1904 Pacelli received his doctorate. The theme of his thesis was the nature of <a href="/wiki/Concordats" class="mw-redirect" title="Concordats">concordats</a> and the function of canon law when a concordat falls into abeyance. Promoted to the position of <i>minutante</i>, he prepared digests of reports that had been sent to the Secretariat from all over the world and in the same year became a <a href="/wiki/Papal_chamberlain" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal chamberlain">papal chamberlain</a>. In 1905 he received the title <a href="/wiki/Domestic_Prelate" class="mw-redirect" title="Domestic Prelate">domestic prelate</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-marchione193_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marchione193-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> From 1904 until 1916, he assisted Cardinal Pietro Gasparri in his codification of <a href="/wiki/Canon_law_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon law (Catholic Church)">canon law</a> with the Department of Extraordinary Ecclesiastical Affairs.<sup id="cite_ref-marchione10_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marchione10-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/John_Cornwell_(writer)" title="John Cornwell (writer)">John Cornwell</a> "the text, together with the <a href="/wiki/Anti-Modernist_Oath" class="mw-redirect" title="Anti-Modernist Oath">Anti-Modernist Oath</a>, became the means by which the Holy See was to establish and sustain the new, unequal, and unprecedented power relationship that had arisen between the papacy and the Church".<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1908, Pacelli served as a Vatican representative on the <a href="/wiki/International_Eucharistic_Congress" class="mw-redirect" title="International Eucharistic Congress">International Eucharistic Congress</a>, accompanying <a href="/wiki/Rafael_Merry_del_Val" title="Rafael Merry del Val">Rafael Merry del Val</a><sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> to London,<sup id="cite_ref-marchione9_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marchione9-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> where he met <a href="/wiki/Winston_Churchill" title="Winston Churchill">Winston Churchill</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> In 1911, he represented the Holy See at the <a href="/wiki/Coronation_of_the_British_monarch" title="Coronation of the British monarch">coronation</a> of King <a href="/wiki/George_V" title="George V">George V</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-marchione10_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marchione10-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
Pacelli became the under-secretary in 1911, adjunct-secretary in 1912 (a position he received under <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pope Pius X</a> and retained under <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XV" title="Pope Benedict XV">Pope Benedict XV</a>), and secretary of the Department of Extraordinary Ecclesiastical Affairs in February 1914.<sup id="cite_ref-marchione10_22-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marchione10-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> On 24 June 1914, just four days before <a href="/wiki/Archduke_Franz_Ferdinand_of_Austria" title="Archduke Franz Ferdinand of Austria">Archduke Franz Ferdinand of Austria</a> was assassinated in <a href="/wiki/Sarajevo" title="Sarajevo">Sarajevo</a>, Pacelli, together with <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Merry_del_Val" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal Merry del Val">Cardinal Merry del Val</a>, represented the Vatican when the Serbian Concordat was signed. <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a>'s success in the <a href="/wiki/First_Balkan_War" title="First Balkan War">First Balkan War</a> against <a href="/wiki/Turkey" title="Turkey">Turkey</a> in 1912 had increased the number of Catholics within greater Serbia. At this time Serbia, encouraged by Russia, was challenging <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a>'s sphere of influence throughout the <a href="/wiki/Balkans" title="Balkans">Balkans</a>. Pius X died on 20 August 1914. His successor Benedict XV named Gasparri as secretary of state and Gasparri took Pacelli with him into the Secretariat of State, making him undersecretary.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> During World War I, Pacelli maintained the Vatican's registry of <a href="/wiki/Prisoner_of_war" title="Prisoner of war">prisoners of war</a> and worked to implement papal relief initiatives. In 1915, he travelled to <a href="/wiki/Vienna" title="Vienna">Vienna</a> to assist <a href="/wiki/Raffaele_Scapinelli_di_L%C3%A9guigno" class="mw-redirect" title="Raffaele Scapinelli di Léguigno">Raffaele Scapinelli</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nuncio" title="Nuncio">nuncio</a> to Vienna, in his negotiations with Emperor <a href="/wiki/Franz_Joseph_I_of_Austria" title="Franz Joseph I of Austria">Franz Joseph I of Austria</a> regarding Italy.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Archbishop_and_papal_nuncio">Archbishop and papal nuncio</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Archbishop and papal nuncio">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Nunciature_of_Eugenio_Pacelli" title="Nunciature of Eugenio Pacelli">Nunciature of Eugenio Pacelli</a></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg/170px-PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="97" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg/255px-PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg/340px-PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg 2x" data-file-width="915" data-file-height="524" /></a><figcaption>Pacelli at the Headquarters of <a href="/wiki/Wilhelm_II,_German_Emperor" title="Wilhelm II, German Emperor">Wilhelm II</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Pope Benedict XV appointed Pacelli as <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Nuncio_to_Bavaria" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Nuncio to Bavaria">nuncio to Bavaria</a> on 23 April 1917, consecrating him as <a href="/wiki/Titular_bishop" title="Titular bishop">titular</a> <a href="/wiki/See_of_Sardis" title="See of Sardis">Archbishop of Sardis</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Sistine_Chapel" title="Sistine Chapel">Sistine Chapel</a> on 13 May 1917, the same day as the <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_F%C3%A1tima" title="Our Lady of Fátima">first apparition of the Blessed Virgin Mary</a> in <a href="/wiki/F%C3%A1tima,_Portugal" title="Fátima, Portugal">Fatima, Portugal</a>. After his consecration, Eugenio Pacelli left for <a href="/wiki/Bavaria" title="Bavaria">Bavaria</a>. As there was no nuncio to <a href="/wiki/Prussia" title="Prussia">Prussia</a> or Germany at the time, Pacelli was, for all practical purposes, the nuncio to all of the <a href="/wiki/German_Empire" title="German Empire">German Empire</a>.
</p><p>Once in <a href="/wiki/Munich" title="Munich">Munich</a>, he conveyed the papal initiative to end the war to German authorities.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> He met with <a href="/wiki/Ludwig_III_of_Bavaria" title="Ludwig III of Bavaria">King Ludwig III</a> on 29 May, and later with <a href="/wiki/Wilhelm_II_of_Germany" class="mw-redirect" title="Wilhelm II of Germany">Kaiser Wilhelm II</a><sup id="cite_ref-marchione11_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marchione11-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> and Chancellor <a href="/wiki/Theobald_von_Bethmann_Hollweg" title="Theobald von Bethmann Hollweg">Theobald von Bethmann Hollweg</a>, who replied positively to the Papal initiative. However, Bethmann Hollweg was forced to resign and the German High Command, hoping for a military victory, delayed the German reply until 20 September.
</p><p>Sister Pascalina later recalled that the Nuncio was heartbroken that the Kaiser turned a "deaf ear to all his proposals". She later wrote, "Thinking back today on that time, when we Germans still all believed that our weapons would be victorious and the Nuncio was deeply sorry that the chance had been missed to save what there was to save, it occurs to me over and over again how clearly he foresaw what was to come. Once as he traced the course of the <a href="/wiki/Rhine" title="Rhine">Rhine</a> with his finger on a map, he said sadly, 'No doubt this will be lost as well'. I did not want to believe it, but here, too, he was to be proved right."<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For the remainder of the Great War, Pacelli concentrated on Benedict's humanitarian efforts<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> especially among Allied POWs in German custody.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> In the upheaval following the Armistice, a disconcerted Pacelli sought Benedict XV's permission to leave Munich, where <a href="/wiki/Kurt_Eisner" title="Kurt Eisner">Kurt Eisner</a> had formed the <a href="/wiki/Free_State_of_Bavaria" class="mw-redirect" title="Free State of Bavaria">Free State of Bavaria</a>, and he left for a while to <a href="/wiki/Rorschach,_St._Gallen" class="mw-redirect" title="Rorschach, St. Gallen">Rorschach</a>, and a tranquil Swiss sanatorium run by nuns. Schioppa, the <i>uditore</i>, was left in Munich.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>"His recovery began with a 'rapport<span style="padding-right:.15em;">'</span>" with the 24-year-old Sister <a href="/wiki/Pascalina_Lehnert" title="Pascalina Lehnert">Pascalina Lehnert</a> – she would soon be transferred to Munich when Pacelli "pulled strings at the highest level".<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When he returned to Munich, following Eisner's assassination by Bavarian nationalist <a href="/wiki/Anton_Graf_von_Arco_auf_Valley" title="Anton Graf von Arco auf Valley">Count Anton von Arco auf Valley</a>, he informed Gasparri-using Schioppa's eye-witness testimony of the chaotic scene at the former royal palace as the trio of <a href="/wiki/Max_Levien" title="Max Levien">Max Levien</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eugen_Levine" class="mw-redirect" title="Eugen Levine">Eugen Levine</a>, and Towia Axelrod sought power: "the scene was indescribable [-] the confusion totally chaotic [-] in the midst of all this, a gang of young women, of dubious appearance, Jews like the rest of them hanging around [-] the boss of this female rabble was Levien's mistress, a young Russian woman, a Jew and a divorcée [-] and it was to her that the nunciature was obliged to pay homage in order to proceed [-] Levien is a young man, also Russian and a Jew. Pale, dirty, with drugged eyes, vulgar, repulsive ..." John Cornwell alleges that a worrying impression of anti-Semitism is discernible in the "catalogue of epithets describing their physical and moral repulsiveness" and Pacelli's "constant harping on the Jewishness of this party of power usurpers" chimed with the "growing and widespread belief among Germans that the Jews were the instigators of the Bolshevik revolution, their principal aim being the destruction of Christian civilization".<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Also according to Cornwell, Pacelli informed Gasparri that "the capital of Bavaria, is suffering under a harsh Jewish-Russian revolutionary tyranny".<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:PacelliBavaria1922a.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/PacelliBavaria1922a.JPG/145px-PacelliBavaria1922a.JPG" decoding="async" width="145" height="203" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/PacelliBavaria1922a.JPG/218px-PacelliBavaria1922a.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/PacelliBavaria1922a.JPG/290px-PacelliBavaria1922a.JPG 2x" data-file-width="716" data-file-height="1002" /></a><figcaption>Pacelli in Bavaria, 1922</figcaption></figure>
<p>According to Sister Pascalina Lehnert, the Nuncio was repeatedly threatened by emissaries of the Bavarian Soviet Republic. Once, in a violation of international law, the Bavarian Revolutionary Government attempted to confiscate the Nunciature's car at gunpoint. Despite their demands, however, Pacelli refused to leave his post.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After the <a href="/wiki/Bavarian_Soviet_Republic" title="Bavarian Soviet Republic">Munich Soviet Republic</a> was defeated and toppled by <a href="/wiki/Freikorps" title="Freikorps">Freikorps</a> and <a href="/wiki/Reichswehr" title="Reichswehr">Reichswehr</a> troops, the Nuncio focused on, according to Lehnert, "alleviating the distress of the postwar period, consoling, supporting all in word and deed".<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bundesarchiv_Bild_102-00535,_Bamberg,_900_Jahr-Feier_der_Stadt.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Bundesarchiv_Bild_102-00535%2C_Bamberg%2C_900_Jahr-Feier_der_Stadt.jpg/220px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_102-00535%2C_Bamberg%2C_900_Jahr-Feier_der_Stadt.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Bundesarchiv_Bild_102-00535%2C_Bamberg%2C_900_Jahr-Feier_der_Stadt.jpg/330px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_102-00535%2C_Bamberg%2C_900_Jahr-Feier_der_Stadt.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Bundesarchiv_Bild_102-00535%2C_Bamberg%2C_900_Jahr-Feier_der_Stadt.jpg/440px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_102-00535%2C_Bamberg%2C_900_Jahr-Feier_der_Stadt.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="520" /></a><figcaption>Nuncio Pacelli in July 1924 at the 900th anniversary of the City of <a href="/wiki/Bamberg" title="Bamberg">Bamberg</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Pacelli was appointed <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Nuncio_to_Germany" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Nuncio to Germany">Apostolic Nuncio to Germany</a> on 23 June 1920, and – after the completion of a Bavarian concordat – his nunciature was moved to Berlin in August 1925. Many of Pacelli's Munich staff stayed with him for the rest of his life, including his advisor <a href="/wiki/Robert_Leiber" title="Robert Leiber">Robert Leiber</a> and Sister <a href="/wiki/Pascalina_Lehnert" title="Pascalina Lehnert">Pascalina Lehnert</a> — housekeeper, cook, friend, and adviser for 41 years. In Berlin, Pacelli was Dean of the Diplomatic Corps and active in diplomatic and many social activities. He was aided by the German priest <a href="/wiki/Ludwig_Kaas" title="Ludwig Kaas">Ludwig Kaas</a>, who was known for his expertise in Church-state relations and was a full-time politician, politically active in the Catholic <a href="/wiki/Centre_Party_(Germany)" title="Centre Party (Germany)">Centre Party</a>, a party he led following <a href="/wiki/Wilhelm_Marx" title="Wilhelm Marx">Wilhelm Marx</a>'s resignation in October 1928.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> While in Germany, he travelled to all regions, attended <a href="/wiki/Katholikentag" title="Katholikentag">Katholikentag</a> (national gatherings of the faithful), and delivered some 50 sermons and speeches to the German people.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> In Berlin he lived in the <a href="/wiki/Tiergarten_(Berlin)" title="Tiergarten (Berlin)">Tiergarten</a> quarter and threw parties for the official and diplomatic elite. <a href="/wiki/Paul_von_Hindenburg" title="Paul von Hindenburg">Paul von Hindenburg</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gustav_Stresemann" title="Gustav Stresemann">Gustav Stresemann</a>, and other members of the Cabinet were regular guests.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Future_Pope_Pius_XII_visits_Dorstfeld_Mines_in_1927_Germany.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Future_Pope_Pius_XII_visits_Dorstfeld_Mines_in_1927_Germany.jpg/220px-Future_Pope_Pius_XII_visits_Dorstfeld_Mines_in_1927_Germany.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Future_Pope_Pius_XII_visits_Dorstfeld_Mines_in_1927_Germany.jpg/330px-Future_Pope_Pius_XII_visits_Dorstfeld_Mines_in_1927_Germany.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Future_Pope_Pius_XII_visits_Dorstfeld_Mines_in_1927_Germany.jpg/440px-Future_Pope_Pius_XII_visits_Dorstfeld_Mines_in_1927_Germany.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1897" data-file-height="1241" /></a><figcaption>Nuncio Pacelli visits the coal mine Dorstfeld on the occasion of the <i><a href="/wiki/Katholikentag" title="Katholikentag">Katholikentag</a></i> in <a href="/wiki/Dortmund" title="Dortmund">Dortmund</a> in 1927 Germany</figcaption></figure>
<p>In post-war Germany, in the absence of a nuncio in Moscow, Pacelli worked also on diplomatic arrangements between the Vatican and the Soviet Union. He negotiated food shipments for Russia, where the Catholic Church was persecuted. He met with Soviet representatives including Foreign Minister <a href="/wiki/Georgi_Chicherin" class="mw-redirect" title="Georgi Chicherin">Georgi Chicherin</a>, who rejected any kind of religious education, the ordination of priests and bishops, but offered agreements without the points vital to the Vatican.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite Vatican pessimism and a lack of visible progress, Pacelli continued the secret negotiations, until <a href="/wiki/Pius_XI" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XI">Pius XI</a> ordered them to be discontinued in 1927. Pacelli supported German diplomatic activity aimed at rejection of punitive measures from victorious former enemies. He blocked French attempts for an ecclesiastical separation of the <a href="/wiki/Saar_(League_of_Nations)" class="mw-redirect" title="Saar (League of Nations)">Saar region</a>, supported the appointment of a papal administrator for <a href="/wiki/Danzig" class="mw-redirect" title="Danzig">Danzig</a> and aided the reintegration of priests expelled from Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> A Prussian Concordat was signed on 14 June 1929. Following the <a href="/wiki/Wall_Street_Crash_of_1929" title="Wall Street Crash of 1929">Wall Street Crash of 1929</a>, the beginnings of a world economic slump appeared, and the days of the <a href="/wiki/Weimar_Republic" title="Weimar Republic">Weimar Republic</a> were numbered. Pacelli was summoned back to Rome at this time — the call coming by telegram when he was resting at his favourite retreat, the Rorschach convent sanatorium. He left Berlin on 10 December 1929.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> David Dalin wrote "of the forty-four speeches Pacelli gave in Germany as papal nuncio between 1917 and 1929, forty denounced some aspect of the emerging Nazi ideology".<sup id="cite_ref-The_Pius_War_2010,_p._17_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Pius_War_2010,_p._17-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> In 1935 he wrote a letter to the bishop of Cologne describing the Nazis as "false prophets with the pride of Lucifer". and as "bearers of a new faith and a new Evangile" who were attempting to create "a mendacious antimony between faithfulness to the Church and the Fatherland".<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Two years later at Notre Dame in Paris he named Germany as "that noble and powerful nation whom bad shepherds would lead astray into an ideology of race".<sup id="cite_ref-The_Pius_War_2010,_p._17_44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Pius_War_2010,_p._17-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cardinal_Secretary_of_State_and_Camerlengo">Cardinal Secretary of State and Camerlengo</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Cardinal Secretary of State and Camerlengo">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_448.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_448.jpg/270px-Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_448.jpg" decoding="async" width="270" height="203" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_448.jpg/405px-Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_448.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_448.jpg/540px-Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_448.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2624" data-file-height="1977" /></a><figcaption>Secretary of State Pacelli with Brazilian president <a href="/wiki/Get%C3%BAlio_Vargas" title="Getúlio Vargas">Getúlio Vargas</a> (at Pacelli's right shoulder) and other dignitaries in <a href="/wiki/Rio_de_Janeiro" title="Rio de Janeiro">Rio de Janeiro</a>, 1934</figcaption></figure>
<p>Pacelli was made a <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholic_Church)" title="Cardinal (Catholic Church)">Cardinal-Priest</a> of <a href="/wiki/Santi_Giovanni_e_Paolo_(Rome)" class="mw-redirect" title="Santi Giovanni e Paolo (Rome)">Santi Giovanni e Paolo</a> on 16 December 1929 by Pope Pius XI, and within a few months, on 7 February 1930, Pius XI appointed him <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a>, responsible for foreign policy and state relations throughout the world. In 1935, Pacelli was named <a href="/wiki/Camerlengo_of_the_Holy_Roman_Church" title="Camerlengo of the Holy Roman Church">Camerlengo of the Holy Roman Church</a>.
</p><p>As Cardinal Secretary of State, Pacelli signed concordats with a number of countries and states. Immediately on becoming Cardinal Secretary of State, Pacelli and Ludwig Kaas took up negotiations on a <a href="/wiki/Baden" title="Baden">Baden</a> Concordat which continued until the spring and summer of 1932. Papal fiat appointed a supporter of Pacelli and his concordat policy, <a href="/wiki/Conrad_Gr%C3%B6ber" title="Conrad Gröber">Conrad Gröber</a>, the new <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Freiburg" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Freiburg">Archbishop of Freiburg</a>, and the treaty was signed in August
1932.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Others followed: Austria (1933), Germany (1933), <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Yugoslavia" title="Kingdom of Yugoslavia">Yugoslavia</a> (1935) and Portugal (1940). The <a href="/wiki/Lateran_treaties" class="mw-redirect" title="Lateran treaties">Lateran treaties</a> with Italy (1929) were concluded before Pacelli became Secretary of State. Catholicism had become the sole recognized religion; the powerful democratic <a href="/wiki/Italian_People%27s_Party_(1919)" title="Italian People&#39;s Party (1919)">Catholic Popular Party</a>, in many ways similar to the Centre Party in Germany, had been disbanded, and in place of political Catholicism the Holy See encouraged <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Action" title="Catholic Action">Catholic Action</a>, "an anaemic form of clerically dominated religious rally-rousing". It was permitted only so long as it developed "its activity outside every political party and in direct dependence upon the Church hierarchy for the dissemination and implementation of Catholic principles".<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> Such concordats allowed the Catholic Church to organize youth groups, make ecclesiastical appointments, run schools, hospitals, and charities, or even conduct religious services. They also ensured that canon law would be recognized within some spheres (e.g., church decrees of <a href="/wiki/Nullity_(conflict)" class="mw-redirect" title="Nullity (conflict)">nullity</a> in the area of marriage).<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As the decade began Pacelli wanted the Centre Party in Germany to turn away from the socialists. In the summer of 1931 he clashed with Catholic chancellor <a href="/wiki/Heinrich_Br%C3%BCning" title="Heinrich Brüning">Heinrich Brüning</a>, who frankly told Pacelli he believed that he "misunderstood the political situation in Germany and the real character of the Nazis".<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Following Brüning's resignation in May 1932 Pacelli, like the new Catholic chancellor <a href="/wiki/Franz_von_Papen" title="Franz von Papen">Franz von Papen</a>, wondered if the Centre Party should look to the Right for a coalition, "that would correspond to their principles".<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> He made many diplomatic visits throughout Europe and the Americas, including an <a href="/wiki/Eugenio_Pacelli%27s_1936_visit_to_the_United_States" title="Eugenio Pacelli&#39;s 1936 visit to the United States">extensive visit to the United States in 1936</a> where he met President <a href="/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt" title="Franklin D. Roosevelt">Franklin D. Roosevelt</a>, who appointed a personal envoy — who did not require Senate confirmation — to the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> in December 1939, re-establishing a diplomatic tradition that had been broken since 1870 when the pope lost <a href="/wiki/Temporal_power_(Papal)" class="mw-redirect" title="Temporal power (Papal)">temporal power</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pacelli_in_Argentina_01.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/Pacelli_in_Argentina_01.jpg/280px-Pacelli_in_Argentina_01.jpg" decoding="async" width="280" height="220" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/4d/Pacelli_in_Argentina_01.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="400" data-file-height="314" /></a><figcaption>A smiling Pacelli with Argentine president <a href="/wiki/Agust%C3%ADn_P._Justo" class="mw-redirect" title="Agustín P. Justo">Agustín P. Justo</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Pacelli presided as <a href="/wiki/Papal_Legate" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal Legate">Papal Legate</a> over the <a href="/wiki/International_Eucharistic_Congress" class="mw-redirect" title="International Eucharistic Congress">International Eucharistic Congress</a> in <a href="/wiki/Buenos_Aires" title="Buenos Aires">Buenos Aires</a>, <a href="/wiki/Argentina" title="Argentina">Argentina</a> in 10–14 October 1934, and in <a href="/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest">Budapest</a> in 25–30 May 1938.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> At this time, <a href="/wiki/Antisemitism" title="Antisemitism">anti-semitic</a> laws were in the process of being formulated in Hungary. Pacelli made reference to the Jews "whose lips curse [Christ] and whose hearts reject him even today".<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Disputed_statement" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:Disputed statement"><span title="This claim has reliable sources with contradicting facts (April 2022)">disputed</span></a>&#32;<span class="metadata"> &#8211; <a href="/wiki/Talk:Pope_Pius_XII#Alleged_reference" title="Talk:Pope Pius XII">discuss</a></span></i>&#93;</sup> This traditional adversarial relationship with Judaism would be reversed in <i><a href="/wiki/Nostra_aetate" title="Nostra aetate">Nostra aetate</a></i> issued during the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Bottum_(author)" title="Joseph Bottum (author)">Joseph Bottum</a>, Pacelli in 1937 "warned A. W. Klieforth, the American consul to Berlin, that <a href="/wiki/Adolf_Hitler" title="Adolf Hitler">Hitler</a> was 'an untrustworthy scoundrel and fundamentally wicked person', to quote Klieforth, who also wrote that Pacelli 'did not believe Hitler capable of moderation, and ... fully supported the German bishops in their anti-Nazi stand'. This was matched with the discovery of Pacelli's <a href="/wiki/Anti-Nazi" class="mw-redirect" title="Anti-Nazi">anti-Nazi</a> report, written the following year for President Roosevelt and filed with Ambassador <a href="/wiki/Joseph_P._Kennedy_Sr." title="Joseph P. Kennedy Sr.">Joseph Kennedy</a>, which declared that the church regarded compromise with the <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Third Reich</a> as 'out of the question'."<sup id="cite_ref-bottum_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bottum-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Historian Walter Bussmann argued that Pacelli, as Cardinal Secretary of State, dissuaded Pope Pius XI – who was nearing death at the time<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> — from condemning the <i><a href="/wiki/Kristallnacht" title="Kristallnacht">Kristallnacht</a></i> in November 1938,<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> when he was informed of it by the papal nuncio in Berlin.<sup id="cite_ref-gutman1136_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gutman1136-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The draft encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Humani_generis_unitas" title="Humani generis unitas">Humani generis unitas</a></i> ("On the Unity of the Human Race") was ready in September 1938 but, according to those responsible for an edition of the document<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> and other sources, it was not forwarded to the Holy See by the Jesuit General <a href="/wiki/Wlodimir_Led%C3%B3chowski" title="Wlodimir Ledóchowski">Wlodimir Ledóchowski</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-tablet_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tablet-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> The draft encyclical contained an open and clear condemnation of <a href="/wiki/Colonialism" title="Colonialism">colonialism</a>, racial persecution and <a href="/wiki/Antisemitism" title="Antisemitism">antisemitism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-tablet_60-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tablet-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources#What_information_to_include" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="A complete citation is needed. (August 2018)">full citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> Historians Passelecq and Suchecky have argued that Pacelli learned about the existence of the draft only after the death of Pius XI and did not promulgate it as Pope.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> He did use parts of it in his inaugural encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Summi_Pontificatus" title="Summi Pontificatus">Summi Pontificatus</a></i>, which he titled "On the Unity of Human Society".<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> His various positions on church and policy issues during his tenure as Cardinal Secretary of State were made public by the Holy See in 1939. Most noteworthy among the 50 speeches is his review of Church-State issues in Budapest in 1938.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Reichskonkordat_and_Mit_brennender_Sorge"><i>Reichskonkordat</i> and <i>Mit brennender Sorge</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Reichskonkordat and Mit brennender Sorge">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Reichskonkordat" title="Reichskonkordat">Reichskonkordat</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mit_brennender_Sorge" title="Mit brennender Sorge">Mit brennender Sorge</a></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:PioXI_et_Pacelliinaugurazioneradiovaticana.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/PioXI_et_Pacelliinaugurazioneradiovaticana.jpg/170px-PioXI_et_Pacelliinaugurazioneradiovaticana.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="263" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/PioXI_et_Pacelliinaugurazioneradiovaticana.jpg/255px-PioXI_et_Pacelliinaugurazioneradiovaticana.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/PioXI_et_Pacelliinaugurazioneradiovaticana.jpg/340px-PioXI_et_Pacelliinaugurazioneradiovaticana.jpg 2x" data-file-width="441" data-file-height="683" /></a><figcaption>Pius XI (center) with Cardinal Pacelli (front left), the radio transmission pioneer <a href="/wiki/Guglielmo_Marconi" title="Guglielmo Marconi">Guglielmo Marconi</a> (back left) and others at the inauguration of <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Radio" title="Vatican Radio">Vatican Radio</a> on 12 February 1931</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-R24391,_Konkordatsunterzeichnung_in_Rom.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-R24391%2C_Konkordatsunterzeichnung_in_Rom.jpg/300px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-R24391%2C_Konkordatsunterzeichnung_in_Rom.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-R24391%2C_Konkordatsunterzeichnung_in_Rom.jpg/450px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-R24391%2C_Konkordatsunterzeichnung_in_Rom.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-R24391%2C_Konkordatsunterzeichnung_in_Rom.jpg/600px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-R24391%2C_Konkordatsunterzeichnung_in_Rom.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="440" /></a><figcaption>Pacelli <i>(seated, center)</i> at the signing of the <i><a href="/wiki/Reichskonkordat" title="Reichskonkordat">Reichskonkordat</a></i> on 20 July 1933 in Rome with <i>(from left to right)</i>: German prelate Ludwig Kaas, German Vice-Chancellor <a href="/wiki/Franz_von_Papen" title="Franz von Papen">Franz von Papen</a>, Secretary of Extraordinary Ecclesiastical Affairs <a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Pizzardo" title="Giuseppe Pizzardo">Giuseppe Pizzardo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alfredo_Ottaviani" title="Alfredo Ottaviani">Alfredo Ottaviani</a>, and Reich minister <a href="/wiki/Rudolf_Buttmann" title="Rudolf Buttmann">Rudolf Buttmann</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The <i>Reichskonkordat</i> was an integral part of four <a href="/wiki/Concordat" title="Concordat">concordats</a> Pacelli concluded on behalf of the Vatican with German States. The state concordats were necessary because the German <a href="/wiki/Federalism" title="Federalism">federalist</a> Weimar constitution gave the German states authority in the area of education and culture and thus diminished the authority of the churches in these areas; this diminution of church authority was a primary concern of the Vatican. As Bavarian Nuncio, Pacelli negotiated successfully with the Bavarian authorities in 1925. He expected the concordat with Catholic Bavaria to be the model for the rest of Germany.<sup id="cite_ref-Volk_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Volk-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> Prussia showed interest in negotiations only after the Bavarian concordat. However, Pacelli obtained less favorable conditions for the church in the Prussian concordat of 1929, which excluded educational issues. A concordat with the German state of Baden was completed by Pacelli in 1932, after he had moved to Rome. There he also negotiated a concordat with Austria in 1933.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> A total of 16 concordats and treaties with European states had been concluded in the ten-year period 1922–1932.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <i>Reichskonkordat</i>, signed on 20 July 1933, between Germany and the Holy See, while thus a part of an overall Vatican policy, was controversial from its beginning. It remains the most important of Pacelli's concordats. It is debated, not because of its content, which is still valid today, but because of its timing. A national concordat with Germany was one of Pacelli's main objectives as secretary of state, because he had hoped to strengthen the legal position of the church. Pacelli, who knew German conditions well, emphasized in particular protection for Catholic associations (§31), freedom for education and Catholic schools, and freedom for publications.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As <a href="/wiki/Nuncio" title="Nuncio">nuncio</a> during the 1920s, he had made unsuccessful attempts to obtain German agreement for such a treaty, and between 1930 and 1933 he attempted to initiate negotiations with representatives of successive German governments, but the opposition of Protestant and Socialist parties, the instability of national governments and the care of the individual states to guard their autonomy thwarted this aim. In particular, the questions of denominational schools and pastoral work in the armed forces prevented any agreement on the national level, despite talks in the winter of 1932.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Adolf_Hitler" title="Adolf Hitler">Adolf Hitler</a> was appointed Chancellor on 30 January 1933 and sought to gain international respectability and to remove internal opposition by representatives of the church and the Catholic <a href="/wiki/Centre_Party_(Germany)" title="Centre Party (Germany)">Centre Party</a>. He sent his vice chancellor <a href="/wiki/Franz_von_Papen" title="Franz von Papen">Franz von Papen</a>, a Catholic nobleman, to Rome to offer negotiations about a Reichskonkordat.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> On behalf of Pacelli, Prelate Ludwig Kaas, the outgoing chairman of the Centre Party, negotiated first drafts of the terms with Papen.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> The concordat was finally signed, by Pacelli for the Vatican and von Papen for Germany, on 20 July and ratified on 10 September 1933.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Konrad_von_Preysing" title="Konrad von Preysing">Bishop Preysing</a> cautioned against compromise with the new regime, against those who saw the Nazi persecution of the church as an aberration that Hitler would correct.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Between 1933 and 1939, Pacelli issued 55 protests of violations of the <i>Reichskonkordat</i>. Most notably, early in 1937, Pacelli asked several German cardinals, including Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Michael_von_Faulhaber" title="Michael von Faulhaber">Michael von Faulhaber</a>, to help him write a protest of Nazi violations of the <i>Reichskonkordat</i>; this was to become Pius XI's 1937 encyclical, <i><a href="/wiki/Mit_brennender_Sorge" title="Mit brennender Sorge">Mit brennender Sorge</a></i>. The encyclical was written in German and not the usual <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a> of official Catholic Church documents. Secretly distributed by an army of motorcyclists and read from every German Catholic Church pulpit on <a href="/wiki/Palm_Sunday" title="Palm Sunday">Palm Sunday</a>, it condemned the <a href="/wiki/Paganism" title="Paganism">paganism</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Nazism" title="Nazism">Nazi</a> ideology.<sup id="cite_ref-Vidmar327_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vidmar327-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Pius XI credited its creation and writing to Pacelli.<sup id="cite_ref-Pham45_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pham45-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> It was the first official denunciation of Nazism made by any major organization and resulted in persecution of the church by the infuriated Nazis who closed all the participating presses and "took numerous vindictive measures against the Church, including staging a long series of immorality trials of the Catholic clergy".<sup id="cite_ref-Bokenkotter389_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bokenkotter389-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> On 10 June 1941, the pope commented on the problems of the <i>Reichskonkordat</i> in a letter to the <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Passau" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Passau">Bishop of Passau</a>, in Bavaria: "The history of the Reichskonkordat shows, that the other side lacked the most basic prerequisites to accept minimal freedoms and rights of the Church, without which the Church simply cannot live and operate, formal agreements notwithstanding".<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relations_with_the_media">Relations with the media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Relations with the media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Cardinal Pacelli gave a lecture entitled "La Presse et L'Apostolat" at the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_University_of_St._Thomas_Aquinas" class="mw-redirect" title="Pontifical University of St. Thomas Aquinas">Pontifical University of St. Thomas Aquinas, <i>Angelicum</i></a> on 17 April 1936.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Papacy">Papacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Papacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Election_and_coronation">Election and coronation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Election and coronation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1939_papal_conclave" title="1939 papal conclave">1939 papal conclave</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: gold;"><span style="font-size:80%">Papal styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">Pope Pius XII</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:C_o_a_Pius_XII.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/C_o_a_Pius_XII.svg/60px-C_o_a_Pius_XII.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="88" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/C_o_a_Pius_XII.svg/90px-C_o_a_Pius_XII.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/C_o_a_Pius_XII.svg/120px-C_o_a_Pius_XII.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="889" data-file-height="1297" /></a></span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/His_Holiness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Holiness">His Holiness</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Your Holiness</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religious style</th><td class="infobox-data">Holy Father</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Posthumous style</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_432.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_432.jpg/220px-Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_432.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="162" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_432.jpg/330px-Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_432.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_432.jpg/440px-Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_432.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2538" data-file-height="1867" /></a><figcaption>Pope Pius XII appears on the central loggia after his election on 2 March 1939</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Piusxii.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Piusxii.jpg/220px-Piusxii.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="78" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/e4/Piusxii.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="267" data-file-height="95" /></a><figcaption>The signature of Pius XII never changed<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>Pope Pius XI died on 10 February 1939. Several historians have interpreted the conclave to choose his successor as facing a choice between a diplomatic or a spiritual candidate, and they view Pacelli's diplomatic experience, especially with Germany, as one of the deciding factors in his election on 2 March 1939, his 63rd birthday, after only one day of deliberation and three ballots.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> He was the first cardinal Secretary of State to be elected pope since <a href="/wiki/Pope_Clement_IX" title="Pope Clement IX">Clement IX</a> in 1667.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> He was one of only two men known to have served as <a href="/wiki/Camerlengo_of_the_Holy_Roman_Church" title="Camerlengo of the Holy Roman Church">Camerlengo</a> immediately prior to being elected as pope (the other being <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a>). According to rumours, he asked for another ballot to be taken to ensure the validity of his election. After his election was indeed confirmed, he chose the name Pius XII in honour of his immediate predecessor.
</p><p>His <a href="/wiki/Papal_coronation" title="Papal coronation">coronation</a> took place on 12 March 1939. Upon being elected pope he was also formally the Grand Master of the Equestrian <a href="/wiki/Order_of_the_Holy_Sepulchre" title="Order of the Holy Sepulchre">Order of the Holy Sepulchre</a> of <a href="/wiki/Jerusalem" title="Jerusalem">Jerusalem</a>, <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith">prefect of the Supreme Sacred Congregation of the Holy Office</a>, <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Oriental_Churches" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Oriental Churches">prefect of the Sacred Congregation for the Oriental Churches</a> and <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_Bishops" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for Bishops">prefect of the Sacred Consistorial Congregation</a>. There was however a Cardinal-Secretary to run these bodies on a day-to-day basis.
</p><p>Pacelli took the same papal name as his predecessor, a title used exclusively by <a href="/wiki/Category:Italian_popes" title="Category:Italian popes">Italian Popes</a>. He was quoted as saying "I call myself Pius; my whole life was under Popes with this name, but especially as a sign of gratitude towards <a href="/wiki/Pius_XI" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XI">Pius XI</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> On 15 December 1937, during his last consistory, Pius XI strongly hinted to the cardinals that he expected Pacelli to be his successor, saying "He is in your midst."<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> He had previously been quoted as saying: "When today the Pope dies, you'll get another one tomorrow, because the Church continues. It would be a much bigger tragedy, if Cardinal Pacelli dies, because there is only one. I pray every day, God may send another one into one of our seminaries, but as of today, there is only one in this world."<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Appointments">Appointments</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Appointments">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>After his election, he made <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Maglione" title="Luigi Maglione">Luigi Maglione</a> his successor as Cardinal Secretary of State. Cardinal Maglione, a seasoned Vatican diplomat, had reestablished diplomatic relations with Switzerland and was for many years nuncio in Paris. Yet, Maglione did not exercise the influence of his predecessor Pacelli, who as Pope continued his close relation with Giovanni Battista Montini (later <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Tardini" title="Domenico Tardini">Domenico Tardini</a>. After the death of Maglione in 1944, Pius left the position open and named Tardini head of its foreign section and Montini head of the internal section.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Tardini and Montini continued serving there until 1953, when Pius XII decided to appoint them cardinals,<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> an honor which both turned down.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> They were then later appointed to be Pro-Secretary with the privilege to wear Episcopal Insignia.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> Tardini continued to be a close co-worker of the Pope until the death of Pius XII, while Montini became archbishop of <a href="/wiki/Milan" title="Milan">Milan</a>, after the death of <a href="/wiki/Alfredo_Ildefonso_Schuster" title="Alfredo Ildefonso Schuster">Alfredo Ildefonso Schuster</a>.
</p><p>Pius XII slowly eroded the Italian monopoly on the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a>; he employed German and <a href="/wiki/Dutch_people" title="Dutch people">Dutch</a> <a href="/wiki/Jesuit" class="mw-redirect" title="Jesuit">Jesuit</a> advisors, Robert Leiber, <a href="/wiki/Augustin_Bea" title="Augustin Bea">Augustin Bea</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sebastian_Tromp" class="mw-redirect" title="Sebastian Tromp">Sebastian Tromp</a>. He also supported the elevation of Americans such as Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Francis_Spellman" title="Francis Spellman">Francis Spellman</a> from a minor to a major role in the church.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> After World War II, Pius XII appointed more non-Italians than any Pope before him. American appointees included <a href="/wiki/Joseph_P._Hurley" class="mw-redirect" title="Joseph P. Hurley">Joseph P. Hurley</a> as regent of the nunciature in Belgrade, <a href="/wiki/Gerald_P._O%27Hara" class="mw-redirect" title="Gerald P. O&#39;Hara">Gerald P. O'Hara</a> as nuncio to <a href="/wiki/Romania" title="Romania">Romania</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Aloisius_Joseph_Muench" title="Aloisius Joseph Muench">Aloisius Joseph Muench</a> as nuncio to Germany. For the first time, numerous young Europeans, Asians and "Americans were trained in various congregations and secretariats within the Vatican for eventual service throughout the world".<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Consistories">Consistories</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Consistories">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_Pius_XII" title="Cardinals created by Pius XII">Cardinals created by Pius XII</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Pius_XII_in_Throne.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Pope_Pius_XII_in_Throne.jpg/220px-Pope_Pius_XII_in_Throne.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="292" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Pope_Pius_XII_in_Throne.jpg/330px-Pope_Pius_XII_in_Throne.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Pope_Pius_XII_in_Throne.jpg/440px-Pope_Pius_XII_in_Throne.jpg 2x" data-file-width="771" data-file-height="1024" /></a><figcaption>One of the first official color portraits of Pius XII, <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr><span style="white-space:nowrap;">&#8201;1939 – 40</span></figcaption></figure>
<p>Only twice in his pontificate did Pius XII hold a <a href="/wiki/Papal_consistory" title="Papal consistory">consistory</a> to create new <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">cardinals</a>, in contrast to Pius XI, who had done so 17 times in as many years. Pius XII chose not to name new cardinals during World War II, and the number of cardinals shrank to 38, with <a href="/wiki/Dennis_Joseph_Dougherty" title="Dennis Joseph Dougherty">Dennis Joseph Dougherty</a> of <a href="/wiki/Philadelphia" title="Philadelphia">Philadelphia</a> being the only living U.S. cardinal. 
</p><p>The first occasion on 18 February 1946 yielded the elevation of a record 32 new cardinals, almost half of the College of Cardinals and reaching the canonical limit of 70 cardinals.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> In the 1946 consistory, Pius XII, while maintaining the maximum size of the College of Cardinals at 70, named cardinals from <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">China</a>, <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Middle_East" title="Middle East">Middle East</a> and increased the number of Cardinals from the Americas, proportionally lessening the Italian influence.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In his second consistory on 12 January 1953, it was expected that his closest co-workers, Msgrs. <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Tardini" title="Domenico Tardini">Domenico Tardini</a> and Giovanni Montini would be elevated<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> and Pius XII informed the assembled cardinals that both of them were originally on the top of his list,<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> but they had turned down the offer, and were rewarded instead with other promotions.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> Both Montini and Tardini would become Cardinals shortly after Pius' death; Montini later became <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a>. The two consistories of 1946 and 1953 brought an end to over five hundred years of Italians constituting a majority of the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With few exceptions, Italian prelates accepted the changes positively; there was no protest movement or open opposition to the internationalization efforts.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Church_reforms">Church reforms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Church reforms">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Liturgy_reforms">Liturgy reforms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Liturgy reforms">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Liturgical_reforms_of_Pope_Pius_XII" title="Liturgical reforms of Pope Pius XII">Liturgical reforms of Pope Pius XII</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mediator_Dei" title="Mediator Dei">Mediator Dei</a>, and <a href="/wiki/General_Roman_Calendar_of_Pope_Pius_XII" title="General Roman Calendar of Pope Pius XII">General Roman Calendar of Pope Pius XII</a></div>
