<p>In his encyclical <i>Mediator Dei</i>, Pius XII links <a href="/wiki/Catholic_liturgy" title="Catholic liturgy">liturgy</a> with the last will of <a href="/wiki/Jesus_Christ" class="mw-redirect" title="Jesus Christ">Jesus Christ</a>.
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>But it is His will, that the worship He instituted and practiced during His life on earth shall continue ever afterwards without intermission. For He has not left mankind an orphan. He still offers us the support of His powerful, unfailing intercession, acting as our "advocate with the Father". He aids us likewise through His Church, where He is present indefectibly as the ages run their course: through the Church which He constituted "the pillar of truth" and dispenser of grace, and which by His sacrifice on the cross, He founded, consecrated and confirmed forever.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>The church has, therefore, according to Pius XII, a common aim with Christ himself, teaching all men the truth, and offering to God a pleasing and acceptable sacrifice. This way, the church re-establishes the unity between the <a href="/wiki/Creator_deity" title="Creator deity">Creator</a> and His creatures.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> The Sacrifice of the Altar, being Christ's own actions, conveys and dispenses divine grace from Christ to the members of the Mystical Body.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Papst_Pius_XII.,_Kr%C3%B6nung_10._Jahrestag.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Papst_Pius_XII.%2C_Kr%C3%B6nung_10._Jahrestag.jpg/220px-Papst_Pius_XII.%2C_Kr%C3%B6nung_10._Jahrestag.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="303" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Papst_Pius_XII.%2C_Kr%C3%B6nung_10._Jahrestag.jpg/330px-Papst_Pius_XII.%2C_Kr%C3%B6nung_10._Jahrestag.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Papst_Pius_XII.%2C_Kr%C3%B6nung_10._Jahrestag.jpg/440px-Papst_Pius_XII.%2C_Kr%C3%B6nung_10._Jahrestag.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2359" data-file-height="3245" /></a><figcaption>Pope Pius XII seated in the <a href="/wiki/Sedia_gestatoria" title="Sedia gestatoria">Sedia gestatoria</a> in 1949</figcaption></figure>
<p>Bishop <a href="/wiki/Carlos_Duarte_Costa" title="Carlos Duarte Costa">Carlos Duarte Costa</a>, a long-time critic of Pius XII's policies during World War II and an opponent of <a href="/wiki/Clerical_celibacy" title="Clerical celibacy">clerical celibacy</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Sacred_language" title="Sacred language">use of Latin as language of the liturgy</a>, was <a href="/wiki/Excommunication_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Excommunication (Catholic Church)">excommunicated</a> by Pius XII on 2 July 1945.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> He later established a schismatic group called "<a href="/wiki/Brazilian_Catholic_Apostolic_Church" title="Brazilian Catholic Apostolic Church">Brazilian Catholic Apostolic Church</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Canon_law_reforms">Canon law reforms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Canon law reforms">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Eastern_canonical_reforms_of_Pius_XII" title="Eastern canonical reforms of Pius XII">Eastern canonical reforms of Pius XII</a></div>
<p>Decentralized authority and increased independence of the Uniate Churches were aimed at in the <a href="/wiki/Canon_Law" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon Law">Canon Law</a>/Codex Iuris Canonici (CIC) reform. In its new constitutions, Eastern Patriarchs were made almost independent from Rome (CIC Orientalis, 1957) Eastern marriage law (CIC Orientalis, 1949), civil law (CIC Orientalis, 1950), laws governing religious associations (CIC Orientalis, 1952) property law (CIC Orientalis, 1952) and other laws. These reforms and writings of Pius XII were intended to establish Eastern Orientals as equal parts of the mystical body of Christ, as explained in the <a href="/wiki/Encyclical" title="Encyclical">encyclical</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Mystici_Corporis_Christi" title="Mystici Corporis Christi">Mystici Corporis Christi</a></i>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Priests_and_religious">Priests and religious</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Priests and religious">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>With the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_constitution" title="Apostolic constitution">Apostolic constitution</a> <i>Sedis Sapientiae</i>, Pius XII added <a href="/wiki/Social_sciences" class="mw-redirect" title="Social sciences">social sciences</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sociology" title="Sociology">sociology</a>, <a href="/wiki/Psychology" title="Psychology">psychology</a> and <a href="/wiki/Social_psychology" title="Social psychology">social psychology</a>, to the pastoral training of future priests. Pius XII emphasised the need to systematically analyze the <a href="/wiki/Psychological" class="mw-redirect" title="Psychological">psychological</a> condition of candidates to the <a href="/wiki/Priesthood" class="mw-redirect" title="Priesthood">priesthood</a> to ensure that they are capable of a life of celibacy and service.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> Pius XII added one year to the theological formation of future priests. He included a "pastoral year", an introduction into the practice of parish work.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius XII wrote in <i><a href="/wiki/Menti_Nostrae" class="mw-redirect" title="Menti Nostrae">Menti Nostrae</a></i> that the call to constant interior reform and Christian <a href="/wiki/Heroism" class="mw-redirect" title="Heroism">heroism</a> means to be above average, to be a living example of Christian virtue. The strict norms governing their lives are meant to make them models of Christian perfection for lay people.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> Bishops are encouraged to look at model saints like <a href="/wiki/Boniface" class="mw-redirect" title="Boniface">Boniface</a>, and Pope Pius X.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> Priests were encouraged to be living examples of the love of Christ and his sacrifice.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Theology">Theology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Theology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Pius_XII" title="Theology of Pope Pius XII">Theology of Pope Pius XII</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Statue_of_Pope_Pius_XII_-_Fatima.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Statue_of_Pope_Pius_XII_-_Fatima.jpg/220px-Statue_of_Pope_Pius_XII_-_Fatima.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="358" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Statue_of_Pope_Pius_XII_-_Fatima.jpg/330px-Statue_of_Pope_Pius_XII_-_Fatima.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Statue_of_Pope_Pius_XII_-_Fatima.jpg/440px-Statue_of_Pope_Pius_XII_-_Fatima.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1754" data-file-height="2855" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/F%C3%A1tima,_Portugal" title="Fátima, Portugal">Fátima</a> Statue of Pope Pius XII, who consecrated Russia and the <a href="/wiki/World" title="World">World</a>: "Just as a few years ago We consecrated the entire human race to the Immaculate Heart of the <a href="/wiki/Veneration_of_Mary_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Veneration of Mary in the Catholic Church">Virgin Mary</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mother_of_God" class="mw-redirect" title="Mother of God">Mother of God</a>, so today We consecrate and in a most special manner We entrust all the peoples of Russia to this Immaculate Heart..."</figcaption></figure>
<p>Pius XII explained the Catholic faith in 41 encyclicals and almost 1000 messages and speeches during his long pontificate. <i><a href="/wiki/Mystici_Corporis_Christi" title="Mystici Corporis Christi">Mystici Corporis Christi</a></i> clarified membership and participation in the church. The encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Divino_afflante_Spiritu" title="Divino afflante Spiritu">Divino afflante Spiritu</a></i> opened the doors for biblical research. His magisterium was far larger and is difficult to summarize. In numerous speeches Catholic teaching is related to various aspects of life, education, medicine, politics, war and peace, the life of saints, <a href="/wiki/Veneration_of_Mary_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Veneration of Mary in the Catholic Church">Mary</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Mother_of_God" class="mw-redirect" title="Mother of God">Mother of God</a>, things eternal and contemporary. Theologically, Pius XII specified the nature of the teaching authority of the Catholic Church. He also gave a new freedom to engage in theological investigations.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Theological_orientation">Theological orientation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Theological orientation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Biblical_research">Biblical research</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Biblical research">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Divino_afflante_Spiritu" title="Divino afflante Spiritu">Divino afflante Spiritu</a></div>
<p>The encyclical <i>Divino afflante Spiritu</i>, published in 1943,<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> emphasized the role of the Bible. Pius XII freed biblical research from previous limitations. He encouraged Christian theologians to revisit original versions of the Bible in <a href="/wiki/Greek_language" title="Greek language">Greek</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hebrew_language" title="Hebrew language">Hebrew</a>. Noting improvements in <a href="/wiki/Archaeology" title="Archaeology">archaeology</a>, the encyclical reversed Pope Leo XIII's encyclical, which had only advocated going back to the original texts to resolve ambiguity in the <a href="/wiki/Latin_Vulgate" class="mw-redirect" title="Latin Vulgate">Latin Vulgate</a>. The encyclical demands a much better understanding of ancient Hebrew history and traditions. It requires bishops throughout the church to initiate <a href="/wiki/Biblical_studies" title="Biblical studies">biblical studies</a> for lay people. The Pontiff also requests a reorientation of Catholic teaching and education, relying much more on sacred scriptures in <a href="/wiki/Sermon" title="Sermon">sermons</a> and religious instruction.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Role_of_theology">Role of theology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Role of theology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>This theological investigative freedom does not, however, extend to all aspects of theology. According to Pius, theologians, employed by the Catholic Church, are assistants, to teach the official teachings of the church and not their own private thoughts. They are free to engage in empirical research, which the church generously supports, but in matters of <a href="/wiki/Morality" title="Morality">morality</a> and religion, they are subjected to the teaching office and authority of the church, the <a href="/wiki/Magisterium" title="Magisterium">Magisterium</a>. "The most noble office of theology is to show how a doctrine defined by the Church is contained in the sources of revelation, ... in that sense in which it has been defined by the Church."<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> The deposit of faith is authentically interpreted not to each of the faithful, not even to theologians, but only to the teaching authority of the Church.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mariology_and_the_dogma_of_the_Assumption">Mariology and the dogma of the Assumption</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Mariology and the dogma of the Assumption">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tizian_041.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Tizian_041.jpg/170px-Tizian_041.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="313" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Tizian_041.jpg/255px-Tizian_041.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Tizian_041.jpg/340px-Tizian_041.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1050" data-file-height="1932" /></a><figcaption>On 1 November 1950, Pius XII defined the dogma of the Assumption (<a href="/wiki/Titian" title="Titian">Titian</a>'s <i>Assunta</i> (1516–1518) pictured).</figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="World_consecration_to_the_Immaculate_Heart_of_Mary">World consecration to the Immaculate Heart of Mary</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: World consecration to the Immaculate Heart of Mary">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII_1942_consecration_to_the_Immaculate_Heart_of_Mary" title="Pope Pius XII 1942 consecration to the Immaculate Heart of Mary">Pope Pius XII 1942 consecration to the Immaculate Heart of Mary</a></div>
<p>As a young boy and in later life, Pacelli was an ardent follower of the Virgin Mary. He was consecrated as a bishop on 13 May 1917, the very first day of the apparitions of <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_F%C3%A1tima" title="Our Lady of Fátima">Our Lady of Fátima</a>. Based on the <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portuguese</a> <a href="/wiki/Christian_mysticism" title="Christian mysticism">mystic</a> <a href="/wiki/Alexandrina_of_Balazar" title="Alexandrina of Balazar">Alexandrina of Balazar</a> requests, he consecrated the world to the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Heart_of_Mary" title="Immaculate Heart of Mary">Immaculate Heart of Mary</a> in 1942. His remains were to be buried in the crypt of Saint Peter's Basilica on the feast day of Our Lady of Fátima, 13 October 1958.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Dogma_of_the_Assumption_of_Mary">Dogma of the Assumption of Mary</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Dogma of the Assumption of Mary">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Munificentissimus_Deus" title="Munificentissimus Deus">Munificentissimus Deus</a> and <a href="/wiki/Assumption_of_Mary" title="Assumption of Mary">Assumption of Mary</a></div>
<p>On 1 November 1950, Pius XII invoked <a href="/wiki/Papal_infallibility" title="Papal infallibility">papal infallibility</a> for the first time since 1854 by defining the dogma of the <a href="/wiki/Assumption_of_Mary" title="Assumption of Mary">Assumption of Mary</a>, namely that she "having completed the course of her earthly life, was assumed body and soul into heavenly glory".<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> To date this is the last time full papal infallibility has been used. The dogma was preceded by the 1946 encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Deiparae_Virginis_Mariae" title="Deiparae Virginis Mariae">Deiparae Virginis Mariae</a></i>, which requested all Catholic bishops to express their opinion on a possible dogmatization. On 8 September 1953, the encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Fulgens_corona" title="Fulgens corona">Fulgens corona</a></i> announced a Marian year for 1954, the centennial of the Dogma of the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> In the encyclical <a href="/wiki/Ad_caeli_reginam" class="mw-redirect" title="Ad caeli reginam">Ad caeli reginam</a> he promulgated the <a href="/wiki/Queenship_of_Mary" class="mw-redirect" title="Queenship of Mary">Queenship of Mary</a> feast.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> <i><a href="/wiki/Mystici_Corporis_Christi" title="Mystici Corporis Christi">Mystici Corporis Christi</a></i> summarizes his <a href="/wiki/Mariology" title="Mariology">mariology</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-EMCC-110_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EMCC-110-124">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> On 15 August 1954, the Feast of the Assumption, he initiated the practice of leading the <a href="/wiki/Angelus" title="Angelus">Angelus</a> every Sunday before address to the crowd assembled at <a href="/wiki/Castel_Gandolfo" title="Castel Gandolfo">Castel Gandolfo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Social_teachings">Social teachings</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Social teachings">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Social_teachings_of_Pope_Pius_XII" title="Social teachings of Pope Pius XII">Social teachings of Pope Pius XII</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:PiusXIISaluspopuli.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/PiusXIISaluspopuli.jpg/220px-PiusXIISaluspopuli.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/PiusXIISaluspopuli.jpg/330px-PiusXIISaluspopuli.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/PiusXIISaluspopuli.jpg/440px-PiusXIISaluspopuli.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1203" data-file-height="891" /></a><figcaption>Coronation of the <a href="/wiki/Salus_Populi_Romani" title="Salus Populi Romani">Salus Populi Romani</a> by Pope Pius XII in 1954</figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Medical_theology">Medical theology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Medical theology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Pius XII delivered numerous speeches to medical professionals and researchers.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> He addressed doctors, nurses, midwives, to detail all aspects of rights and dignity of patients, medical responsibilities, moral implications of psychological illnesses and the uses of psycho pharmaca. He also took on issues like the uses of medicine in <a href="/wiki/Terminally_ill" class="mw-redirect" title="Terminally ill">terminally ill</a> persons, medical lies in face of grave illness, and the rights of family members to make decisions against expert medical advice. Pope Pius XII often reconsidered previously accepted truth, thus he was first to determine that the use of pain medicine in terminally ill patients is justified, even if this may shorten the life of the patient, as long as life shortening is not the objective itself.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Family_and_sexuality">Family and sexuality</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Family and sexuality">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Pope Pius XII developed an extensive theology of the family, taking issue with family roles, sharing of household duties, education of children, conflict resolution, financial dilemmas, psychological problems, illness, taking care of older generations, unemployment, marital holiness and virtue, common prayer, religious discussions and more. He accepted the <a href="/wiki/Calendar-based_contraceptive_methods" title="Calendar-based contraceptive methods">rhythm method</a> as a moral form of <a href="/wiki/Family_planning" title="Family planning">family planning</a>, although only in limited circumstances, within the context of family.<sup id="cite_ref-midwives_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-midwives-128">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Theology_and_science">Theology and science</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Theology and science">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>To Pius XII, science and religion were heavenly sisters, different manifestations of divine exactness, who could not possibly contradict each other over the long term.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> Regarding their relation, his advisor Professor Robert Leiber wrote: "Pius XII was very careful not to close any doors prematurely. He was energetic on this point and regretted that in the case of <a href="/wiki/Galileo" class="mw-redirect" title="Galileo">Galileo</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Evolution_of_the_human_body">Evolution of the human body</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Evolution of the human body">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Humani_generis" title="Humani generis">Humani generis</a></div>
<p>In 1950, Pius XII promulgated <i>Humani generis</i>, which acknowledged that <a href="/wiki/Evolution" title="Evolution">evolution</a> might accurately describe the biological origins of the human form, but at the same time criticized those who "imprudently and indiscreetly hold that evolution&#160;... explains the origin of all things". Catholics must believe that the human soul was created immediately by God. Since the soul is a spiritual substance, it is not brought into being through transformation of matter, but directly by God, whence the special uniqueness of each person.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Fifty years later, <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>, stating that scientific evidence now seemed to favour the evolutionary theory, upheld the distinction of Pius XII regarding the human soul. "Even if the human body originates from pre-existent living matter, the spiritual soul is spontaneously created by God."<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Capital_punishment">Capital punishment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Capital punishment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In an address given on 14 September 1952, Pope Pius XII said that the church does not regard the execution of criminals as a violation by the State of the universal right to life:
</p>
<blockquote><p>When it is a question of the execution of a condemned man, the State does not dispose of the individual's right to life. In this case it is reserved to the public power to deprive the condemned person of the enjoyment of life in expiation of his crime when, by his crime, he has already disposed himself of his right to live.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>The church regards criminal penalties as both "medicinal", preventing the criminal from re-offending, and "vindictive", providing retribution for the offense committed. Pius defended the authority of the State to carry out punishment, up to and including the death penalty.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Democracy_and_monarchy">Democracy and monarchy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Democracy and monarchy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Pius XII taught that the masses were a threat to true democracy. In such a democracy, liberty is the individual's moral duty and equality is the right of all people to honorably live in the place and station that God has assigned them.<sup id="cite_ref-Pius1944_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pius1944-135">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 1 June 1946, one day before the <a href="/wiki/1946_Italian_institutional_referendum" title="1946 Italian institutional referendum">1946 Italian referendum</a> on whether to abolish or keep the Italian monarchy, Pius XII delivered a sermon on St. Peter's Square. While he did not directly mention monarchy or republicanism, given the context, his speech was nonetheless widely seen as endorsing <a href="/wiki/Umberto_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Umberto II">Umberto II</a> in the referendum, with it being difficult to misunderstand his plea.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius stated:
</p>
<blockquote><p>"The problem is whether one or the other of those nations, of those two Latin sisters [elections were taking place in France on the same day] with several thousands of years of civilization will continue to learn against the solid rock of Christianity,...or on the contrary do they want to hand over the fate of their future to the impossible omnipotence of a material state without extraterrestrial ideals, without religion, and without God. One of these two alternatives shall occur according to whether the names of the champions or the destroyers of Christian civilization emerge victorious from the urns".<sup id="cite_ref-Giuseppe_Mammarealla_p._114_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Giuseppe_Mammarealla_p._114-137">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>After the referendum was successful and the Italian monarchy was abolished, Pius privately agreed with his envoy <a href="/wiki/Myron_Charles_Taylor" title="Myron Charles Taylor">Myron Charles Taylor</a> "...that it would have been far preferable for Italy to remain a monarchy, but he also noted that what was done was done".<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Encyclicals.2C_writings_and_speeches"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Encyclicals,_writings_and_speeches">Encyclicals, writings and speeches</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Encyclicals, writings and speeches">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/List_of_encyclicals_of_Pope_Pius_XII" title="List of encyclicals of Pope Pius XII">List of encyclicals of Pope Pius XII</a> and <a href="/wiki/Magisterium_of_Pius_XII" title="Magisterium of Pius XII">Magisterium of Pius XII</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Our_Lady_of_Good_Counsel_by_Pasquale_Sarullo.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Our_Lady_of_Good_Counsel_by_Pasquale_Sarullo.jpg/170px-Our_Lady_of_Good_Counsel_by_Pasquale_Sarullo.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="251" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Our_Lady_of_Good_Counsel_by_Pasquale_Sarullo.jpg/255px-Our_Lady_of_Good_Counsel_by_Pasquale_Sarullo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Our_Lady_of_Good_Counsel_by_Pasquale_Sarullo.jpg/340px-Our_Lady_of_Good_Counsel_by_Pasquale_Sarullo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="773" data-file-height="1140" /></a><figcaption>In 1939 Pius XII placed his pontificate under the maternal care of <i><a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Good_Counsel" title="Our Lady of Good Counsel">Our Lady of Good Counsel</a></i> and composed a prayer to her.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> This 19th-century painting is by <a href="/wiki/Pasquale_Sarullo" title="Pasquale Sarullo">Pasquale Sarullo</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Pius XII issued 41 <a href="/wiki/Encyclical" title="Encyclical">encyclicals</a> during his pontificate — more than all his successors in the past 50 years taken together — along with many other writings and speeches. The pontificate of Pius XII was the first in Vatican history that published papal speeches and addresses in <a href="/wiki/Vernacular" title="Vernacular">vernacular</a> language on a systematic basis. Until then, papal documents were issued mainly in <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/Acta_Apostolicae_Sedis" title="Acta Apostolicae Sedis">Acta Apostolicae Sedis</a></i> since 1909. Because of the novelty of it all, and a feared <a href="/wiki/Military_occupation" title="Military occupation">occupation</a> of the Vatican by the German <a href="/wiki/Wehrmacht" title="Wehrmacht">Wehrmacht</a>, not all documents exist today. In 1944, a number of papal documents were burned or "walled in".<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>Several encyclicals addressed the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Catholic_Churches" title="Eastern Catholic Churches">Eastern Catholic Churches</a>. <i><a href="/wiki/Orientalis_Ecclesiae" title="Orientalis Ecclesiae">Orientalis Ecclesiae</a></i> was issued in 1944 on the 15th centenary of the death of <a href="/wiki/Cyril_of_Alexandria" title="Cyril of Alexandria">Cyril of Alexandria</a>, a saint common to <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Christianity" title="Eastern Christianity">Eastern Christianity</a> and <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a> Churches. Pius XII asks for prayer for better understanding and unification of the churches. <i><a href="/wiki/Orientales_omnes_Ecclesias" title="Orientales omnes Ecclesias">Orientales omnes Ecclesias</a></i>, issued in 1945 on the 350th anniversary of the reunion, is a call to continued unity of the <a href="/wiki/Ruthenian_Greek_Catholic_Church" title="Ruthenian Greek Catholic Church">Ruthenian Greek Catholic Church</a>, threatened in its very existence by the authorities of the Soviet Union. <i><a href="/wiki/Sempiternus_Rex" title="Sempiternus Rex">Sempiternus Rex</a></i> was issued in 1951 on the 1500th anniversary of the Ecumenical <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Chalcedon" title="Council of Chalcedon">Council of Chalcedon</a>. It included a call to oriental communities adhering to <a href="/wiki/Miaphysite" class="mw-redirect" title="Miaphysite">Miaphysite</a> theology to return to the Catholic Church. <i>Orientales Ecclesias</i> was issued in 1952 and addressed to the Eastern Churches, protesting the continued <a href="/wiki/Stalinist" class="mw-redirect" title="Stalinist">Stalinist</a> persecution of the church. Several <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Letter" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Letter">Apostolic Letters</a> were sent to the bishops in the East. On 13 May 1956, Pope Pius addressed all bishops of the Eastern Rite. Mary, the mother of God, was the subject of encyclical letters to the people of Russia in <i><a href="/wiki/Fulgens_corona" title="Fulgens corona">Fulgens corona</a></i>, as well as a papal letter to the people of Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius XII made two substantial interventions on the media. His 1955 discourse <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=The_Ideal_Movie&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="The Ideal Movie (page does not exist)">The Ideal Movie</a></i>, originally given in two parts to members of the Italian cinema industry, offered a "sophisticated analysis of the film industry and the role of cinema in modern society".<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> Compared to his predecessor's teaching, the encyclical <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Miranda_Prorsus&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Miranda Prorsus (page does not exist)">Miranda Prorsus</a></i> (1957) shows a "high regard for the importance of cinema, television, and radio".<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Feasts_and_devotions">Feasts and devotions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Feasts and devotions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1958, Pope Pius XII declared the Feast of the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Face_of_Jesus" title="Holy Face of Jesus">Holy Face of Jesus</a> as <a href="/wiki/Shrove_Tuesday" title="Shrove Tuesday">Shrove Tuesday</a> (the Tuesday before <a href="/wiki/Ash_Wednesday" title="Ash Wednesday">Ash Wednesday</a>) for all Catholics. The first medal of the Holy Face, produced by Sister <a href="/wiki/Maria_Pierina" class="mw-redirect" title="Maria Pierina">Maria Pierina De Micheli</a>, based on the image on the <a href="/wiki/Shroud_of_Turin" title="Shroud of Turin">Shroud of Turin</a> had been offered to Pius XII who approved the medal and the devotion based on it. The general devotion to the Holy Face of Jesus had been approved by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a> in 1885 before the image on the Turin Shroud had been photographed.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonisations_and_beatifications">Canonisations and beatifications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Canonisations and beatifications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Saints_canonized_by_Pope_Pius_XII" class="mw-redirect" title="Saints canonized by Pope Pius XII">Saints canonized by Pope Pius XII</a> and <a href="/wiki/Beatifications_of_Pope_Pius_XII" title="Beatifications of Pope Pius XII">Beatifications of Pope Pius XII</a></div>
<p>Pope Pius XII <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> numerous people, including Pope <a href="/wiki/Pius_X" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius X">Pius X</a> — "both were determined to stamp out, as far as possible, all traces of dangerous <a href="/wiki/Heterodoxy" title="Heterodoxy">heterodoxy</a>"<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> — and <a href="/wiki/Maria_Goretti" title="Maria Goretti">Maria Goretti</a>. He beatified <a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_XI" title="Pope Innocent XI">Pope Innocent XI</a>. The first canonizations were two women, the founder of a congregation for women, <a href="/wiki/Mary_Euphrasia_Pelletier" title="Mary Euphrasia Pelletier">Mary Euphrasia Pelletier</a>, and a young laywoman, <a href="/wiki/Gemma_Galgani" title="Gemma Galgani">Gemma Galgani</a>. Pelletier had a reputation for opening new ways for Catholic charities, helping people in difficulties with the law, who had been neglected by the system and the church. Galgani was a virtuous woman in her twenties, said to have the <a href="/wiki/Stigmata" title="Stigmata">stigmata</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius XII also named <a href="/wiki/Anthony_of_Padua" title="Anthony of Padua">Anthony of Padua</a> as a <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_the_Church" title="Doctor of the Church">Doctor of the Church</a> on 16 January 1946 while conferring upon him the title <i>Doctor evangelius</i>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_II">World War II</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: World War II">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City_during_World_War_II" title="Vatican City during World War II">Vatican City during World War II</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Pius_XII_and_the_German_Resistance" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XII and the German Resistance">Pius XII and the German Resistance</a>, <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_and_Nazi_Germany" title="Catholic Church and Nazi Germany">Catholic Church and Nazi Germany</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pius_Wars" title="Pius Wars">Pius Wars</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Members_of_the_Royal_22e_Regiment_in_audience_with_Pope_Pius_XII.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Members_of_the_Royal_22e_Regiment_in_audience_with_Pope_Pius_XII.jpg/220px-Members_of_the_Royal_22e_Regiment_in_audience_with_Pope_Pius_XII.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="158" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Members_of_the_Royal_22e_Regiment_in_audience_with_Pope_Pius_XII.jpg/330px-Members_of_the_Royal_22e_Regiment_in_audience_with_Pope_Pius_XII.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Members_of_the_Royal_22e_Regiment_in_audience_with_Pope_Pius_XII.jpg/440px-Members_of_the_Royal_22e_Regiment_in_audience_with_Pope_Pius_XII.jpg 2x" data-file-width="633" data-file-height="455" /></a><figcaption>Members of the Canadian <a href="/wiki/Royal_22e_Regiment" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal 22e Regiment">Royal 22<sup>e</sup> Regiment</a>, in audience with Pope Pius XII, following the 1944 Liberation of Rome</figcaption></figure>
<p>During World War II Pius saw his primary obligation as being to ensure the continuation of the "<a href="/wiki/Church_visible" title="Church visible">Church visible</a>" and its divine mission.<sup id="cite_ref-oxf_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-oxf-155">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> Pius XII lobbied world leaders to prevent the outbreak of World War II and then expressed his dismay that war had come in his October 1939 <i><a href="/wiki/Summi_Pontificatus" title="Summi Pontificatus">Summi Pontificatus</a></i> encyclical. He followed a strict public policy of Vatican neutrality for the duration of the conflict mirroring that of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XV" title="Pope Benedict XV">Pope Benedict XV</a>.
</p><p>In 1939, Pius XII turned the Vatican into a centre of aid which he organized from various parts of the world.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> At the request of the Pope, an information office for prisoners of war and refugees operated in the Vatican under <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Battista_Montini" class="mw-redirect" title="Giovanni Battista Montini">Giovanni Battista Montini</a>, which in the years of its existence from 1939 until 1947 received almost 10&#160;million (9,891,497) information requests and produced over 11&#160;million (11,293,511) answers about missing persons.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>McGoldrick (2012) concludes that during the war:
</p>
<blockquote><p>Pius XII had genuine affection for Germany, though not the criminal element into whose hands it had fallen; he feared Bolshevism, an ideology dedicated to the annihilation of the church of which he was head, but his sympathies lay with the Allies and the democracies, especially the United States, into whose war economy he had transferred and invested the Vatican's considerable assets.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Outbreak_of_war">Outbreak of war</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Outbreak of war">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Summi_Pontificatus">Summi Pontificatus</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Summi Pontificatus">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p><i><a href="/wiki/Summi_Pontificatus" title="Summi Pontificatus">Summi Pontificatus</a></i> was the first papal encyclical issued by Pope Pius XII, in October 1939 and established some of the themes of his pontificate. During the drafting of the letter, the Second World War commenced with the German/Soviet invasion of Catholic Poland — the "dread tempest of war is already raging despite all Our efforts to avert it". The papal letter denounced antisemitism, war, totalitarianism, the attack on Poland and the Nazi persecution of the church.<sup id="cite_ref-vatican.va_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vatican.va-159">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius XII reiterated church teaching on the "principle of equality" — with specific reference to Jews: "there is neither Gentile nor Jew, circumcision nor uncircumcision".<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> The forgetting of solidarity "imposed by our common origin and by the equality of rational nature in all men" was called "pernicious error".<sup id="cite_ref-Pius_XII_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pius_XII-161">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Catholics everywhere were called upon to offer "compassion and help" to the victims of the war.<sup id="cite_ref-http_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-http-162">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> The Pope declared determination to work to hasten the return of peace and trust in prayers for justice, love and mercy, to prevail against the scourge of war.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> The letter also decried the deaths of noncombatants.<sup id="cite_ref-Pius_XII_1939_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pius_XII_1939-164">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following themes addressed in <a href="/wiki/Non_abbiamo_bisogno" title="Non abbiamo bisogno">Non abbiamo bisogno</a> (1931); <a href="/wiki/Mit_brennender_Sorge" title="Mit brennender Sorge">Mit brennender Sorge</a> (1937) and <a href="/wiki/Divini_redemptoris" class="mw-redirect" title="Divini redemptoris">Divini redemptoris</a> (1937), Pius wrote against "anti-Christian movements" and needing to bring back to the church those who were following "a false standard&#160;... misled by error, passion, temptation and prejudice, [who] have strayed away from faith in the true God".<sup id="cite_ref-vatican_165-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vatican-165">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> Pius wrote of "Christians unfortunately more in name than in fact" having shown "cowardice" in the face of persecution by these creeds, and endorsed resistance:<sup id="cite_ref-vatican_165-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vatican-165">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Who among "the Soldiers of Christ"&#160;– ecclesiastic or layman&#160;– does not feel himself incited and spurred on to a greater vigilance, to a more determined resistance, by the sight of the ever-increasing host of Christ's enemies; as he perceives the spokesmen of these tendencies deny or in practice neglect the vivifying truths and the values inherent in belief in God and in Christ; as he perceives them wantonly break the Tables of God's Commandments to substitute other tables and other standards stripped of the ethical content of the Revelation on Sinai, standards in which the spirit of the Sermon on the Mount and of the Cross has no place?</p></blockquote>
<p>Pius wrote of a persecuted church<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> and a time requiring "charity" for victims who had a "right" to compassion. Against the invasion of Poland and killing of civilians he wrote:<sup id="cite_ref-vatican.va_159-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vatican.va-159">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>[This is an] "Hour of Darkness"...&#160;in which the spirit of violence and of discord brings indescribable suffering on mankind... The nations swept into the tragic whirlpool of war are perhaps as yet only at the "beginnings of sorrows"...&#160;but even now there reigns in thousands of families death and desolation, lamentation and misery. The blood of countless human beings, even noncombatants, raises a piteous dirge over a nation such as Our dear Poland, which, for its fidelity to the Church, for its services in the defense of Christian civilization, written in indelible characters in the annals of history, has a right to the generous and brotherly sympathy of the whole world, while it awaits, relying on the powerful intercession of Mary, Help of Christians, the hour of a resurrection in harmony with the principles of justice and true peace.</p></blockquote>
<p>With Italy not yet an ally of Hitler in the war, Italians were called upon to remain faithful to the Catholic Church. Pius avoided explicit denunciations of <a href="/wiki/Hitlerism" class="mw-redirect" title="Hitlerism">Hitlerism</a> or <a href="/wiki/Stalinism" title="Stalinism">Stalinism</a>, establishing the "impartial" public tone which would become controversial in later assessment of his pontificate: "A full statement of the doctrinal stand to be taken in face of the errors of today, if necessary, can be put off to another time unless there is disturbance by calamitous external events; for the moment We limit Ourselves to some fundamental observations."<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Invasion_of_Poland">Invasion of Poland</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Invasion of Poland">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In <i><a href="/wiki/Summi_Pontificatus" title="Summi Pontificatus">Summi Pontificatus</a></i>, Pius expressed dismay at the killing of non-combatants in the Nazi/Soviet invasion of Poland and expressed hope for the "resurrection" of that country. The Nazis and Soviets commenced a <a href="/wiki/Nazi_persecution_of_the_Catholic_Church_in_Poland" title="Nazi persecution of the Catholic Church in Poland">persecution of the Catholic Church in Poland</a>. In April 1940, the Vatican advised the US government that its efforts to provide humanitarian aid had been blocked by the Germans and that the Holy See had been forced to seek indirect channels through which to direct its aid.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Michael_Phayer" title="Michael Phayer">Michael Phayer</a>, a critic of Pius XII, assesses his policy as having been to "refuse to censure" the <a href="/wiki/Invasion_of_Poland" title="Invasion of Poland">"German" invasion and annexation of Poland</a>. This, Phayer wrote, was regarded as a "betrayal" by many Polish Catholics and clergy, who saw his appointment of <a href="/wiki/Hilarius_Breitinger" title="Hilarius Breitinger">Hilarius Breitinger</a> as the apostolic administrator for the <a href="/wiki/Reichsgau_Wartheland" title="Reichsgau Wartheland">Wartheland</a> in May 1942, an "implicit recognition" of the breakup of Poland; the opinions of the <i><a href="/wiki/Volksdeutsche" title="Volksdeutsche">Volksdeutsche</a></i>, mostly German Catholic minorities living in occupied Poland, were more mixed.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> Phayer argues that Pius XII — both before and during his papacy – consistently "deferred to Germany at the expense of Poland", and saw Germany — not Poland — as critical to "rebuilding a large Catholic presence in Central Europe".<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> In May 1942, <a href="/wiki/Kazimierz_Pap%C3%A9e" title="Kazimierz Papée">Kazimierz Papée</a>, Polish ambassador to the Vatican, complained that Pius had failed to condemn the recent wave of atrocities in Poland; when Cardinal Secretary of State Maglione replied that the Vatican could not document individual atrocities, Papée declared, "when something becomes notorious, proof is not required".<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> Although Pius XII received frequent reports about atrocities committed by and/or against Catholics, his knowledge was incomplete; for example, he wept after the war on learning that <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Hlond" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal Hlond">Cardinal Hlond</a> had banned German liturgical services in Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There was a well-known case of Jewish Rabbis who, seeking support against the Nazi persecution of Polish Jews in the <a href="/wiki/General_Government" title="General Government">General Government</a> (Nazi-occupied Polish zone), complained to the representatives of the Catholic Church. The church's attempted intervention caused the Nazis to retaliate by arresting rabbis and deporting them to the death camp. Subsequently, the Catholic Church in Poland abandoned direct intervention, instead focusing on organizing underground aid, with huge international support orchestrated by Pope Pius XII and his Holy See. The Pope was informed about <a href="/wiki/War_crimes_in_occupied_Poland_during_World_War_II" title="War crimes in occupied Poland during World War II">Nazi atrocities committed in Poland</a> by both officials of the Polish Church and the <a href="/wiki/Polish_Underground" class="mw-redirect" title="Polish Underground">Polish Underground</a>. Those intelligence materials were used by Pius XII on 11 March 1940 during a formal audience with <a href="/wiki/Joachim_von_Ribbentrop" title="Joachim von Ribbentrop">Joachim von Ribbentrop</a> (Hitler's foreign affairs adviser) when Pope was "listing the date, place, and precise details of each crime" as described by <a href="/wiki/Joseph_L._Lichten" title="Joseph L. Lichten">Joseph L. Lichten</a><sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> after others.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_actions_to_end_conflict">Early actions to end conflict</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Early actions to end conflict">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>With Poland overrun, but France and the Low Countries yet to be attacked, Pius continued to hope for a negotiated peace to prevent the spread of the conflict. The similarly-minded US President <a href="/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt" title="Franklin D. Roosevelt">Franklin D. Roosevelt</a> re-established American diplomatic relations with the Vatican after a seventy-year hiatus and dispatched <a href="/wiki/Myron_C._Taylor" class="mw-redirect" title="Myron C. Taylor">Myron C. Taylor</a> as his personal representative.<sup id="cite_ref-docs.fdrlibrary.marist.edu_174-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-docs.fdrlibrary.marist.edu-174">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> Pius warmly welcomed Roosevelt's envoy and peace initiative, calling it "an exemplary act of fraternal and hearty solidarity... in defence against the chilling breath of aggressive and deadly godless anti-Christian tendencies".<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> American correspondence spoke of "parallel endeavours for peace and the alleviation of suffering".<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> Despite the early collapse of peace hopes, the Taylor mission continued at the Vatican.<sup id="cite_ref-docs.fdrlibrary.marist.edu_174-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-docs.fdrlibrary.marist.edu-174">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to Hitler biographer <a href="/wiki/John_Toland_(author)" class="mw-redirect" title="John Toland (author)">John Toland</a>, following the November 1939 assassination attempt by <a href="/wiki/Johann_Georg_Elser" class="mw-redirect" title="Johann Georg Elser">Johann Georg Elser</a>, Hitler said Pius would have wanted the plot to succeed: "he's no friend of mine".<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> In the spring of 1940, a group of German generals seeking to overthrow Hitler and make peace with the British approached Pope Pius XII, who acted as an interlocutor between the British and the abortive plot.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> According to Toland, Munich lawyer, <a href="/wiki/Josef_M%C3%BCller_(CSU_politician)" title="Josef Müller (CSU politician)">Joseph Muller</a>, made a clandestine trip to Rome in October 1939, met with Pius XII and found him willing to act as intermediary. The Vatican agreed to send a letter outlining the bases for peace with England and the participation of the Pope was used to try to persuade senior German Generals <a href="/wiki/Franz_Halder" title="Franz Halder">Franz Halder</a> and <a href="/wiki/Walther_von_Brauchitsch" title="Walther von Brauchitsch">Walther von Brauchitsch</a> to act against Hitler.<sup id="cite_ref-John_Toland_p.760_179-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-John_Toland_p.760-179">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius warned the Allies of the planned German invasion of the Low Countries in 1940.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> In Rome in 1942, US envoy Myron C. Taylor, thanked the Holy See for the "forthright and heroic expressions of indignation made by Pope Pius XII when Germany invaded the Low countries".<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup> After Germany invaded the <a href="/wiki/Low_Countries" title="Low Countries">Low Countries</a> during 1940, Pius XII sent expressions of sympathy to the <a href="/wiki/Wilhelmina_of_the_Netherlands" title="Wilhelmina of the Netherlands">Queen of the Netherlands</a>, the <a href="/wiki/King_of_Belgium" class="mw-redirect" title="King of Belgium">King of Belgium</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand Duchess of Luxembourg">Grand Duchess of Luxembourg</a>. When Mussolini learned of the warnings and the telegrams of sympathy, he took them as a personal affront and had his ambassador to the Vatican file an official protest, charging that Pius XII had taken sides against Italy's ally Germany. Mussolini's foreign minister claimed that Pius XII was "ready to let himself be deported to a concentration camp, rather than do anything against his conscience".<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When in 1940, the Nazi Foreign Minister von Ribbentrop led the only senior Nazi delegation permitted an audience with Pius XII and asked why the Pope had sided with the Allies, Pius replied with a list of recent Nazi atrocities and religious persecutions committed against Christians and Jews, in Germany, and in Poland, leading the New York Times to headline its report "Jews Rights Defended" and write of "burning words he spoke to Herr Ribbentrop about religious persecution".<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> During the meeting, Ribbentrop suggested an overall settlement between the Vatican and the Reich government in exchange for Pius XII instructing the German bishops to refrain from political criticism of the German government, but no agreement was reached.<sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bernardinonogara.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Bernardinonogara.jpg/170px-Bernardinonogara.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="219" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/dd/Bernardinonogara.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="228" data-file-height="294" /></a><figcaption>The investments of <a href="/wiki/Bernardino_Nogara" title="Bernardino Nogara">Bernardino Nogara</a> were critical to the financing of the papacy during World War II.</figcaption></figure>
<p>At a special mass at St Peters for the victims of the war, held in November 1940, soon after the commencement of the <a href="/wiki/London_Blitz" class="mw-redirect" title="London Blitz">London Blitz</a> bombing by the <a href="/wiki/Luftwaffe" title="Luftwaffe">Luftwaffe</a>, Pius preached in his homily: "may the whirlwinds, that in the light of day or the dark of night, scatter terror, fire, destruction, and slaughter on helpless folk cease. May justice and charity on one side and on the other be in perfect balance, so that all injustice be repaired, the reign of right restored".<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> Later he appealed to the Allies to spare Rome from aerial bombing, and visited wounded victims of the <a href="/wiki/Bombing_of_Rome_in_World_War_II" title="Bombing of Rome in World War II">Allied bombing of 19 July 1943</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-186">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Widening_conflict">Widening conflict</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Widening conflict">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Pius attempted, unsuccessfully, to dissuade the Italian Dictator <a href="/wiki/Benito_Mussolini" title="Benito Mussolini">Benito Mussolini</a> from joining Hitler in the war.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> In April 1941, Pius XII granted a private audience to <a href="/wiki/Ante_Paveli%C4%87" title="Ante Pavelić">Ante Pavelić</a>, the leader of the <a href="/wiki/Independent_State_of_Croatia" title="Independent State of Croatia">newly proclaimed Croatian state</a> (rather than the diplomatic audience Pavelić had wanted).<sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> Pius was criticised for his reception of Pavelić: an unattributed British <a href="/wiki/Foreign_Office" class="mw-redirect" title="Foreign Office">Foreign Office</a> memo on the subject described Pius as "the greatest moral coward of our age".<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> The Vatican did not officially recognise Pavelić's regime. Pius XII did not publicly condemn the expulsions and forced conversions to Catholicism perpetrated on Serbs by Pavelić;<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> however, the Holy See did expressly repudiate the forced conversions in a memorandum dated 25 January 1942, from the Vatican Secretariat of State to the Yugoslavian Legation.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> The pope was well-informed of <a href="/wiki/Catholic_clergy_involvement_with_the_Usta%C5%A1e" title="Catholic clergy involvement with the Ustaše">Catholic clergy involvement with the Ustaše</a> regime, even possessing a list of clergymembers who had "joined in the slaughter", but decided against condemning the regime or taking action against the clergy involved, fearing that it would lead to schism in the Croatian church or undermine the formation of a future Croatian state.<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> Pius XII would elevate <a href="/wiki/Aloysius_Stepinac" title="Aloysius Stepinac">Aloysius Stepinac</a> — a Croatian archbishop convicted of collaborating with the <a href="/wiki/Usta%C5%A1e" title="Ustaše">Ustaše</a> by the newly established <a href="/wiki/Yugoslav_Communist_regime" class="mw-redirect" title="Yugoslav Communist regime">Yugoslav Communist regime</a> — to the cardinalate in 1953.<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> Phayer agrees that Stepinac's was a "show trial", but states "the charge that he [Pius XII] supported the Ustaša regime was, of course, true, as everyone knew",<sup id="cite_ref-phayerpav_194-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-phayerpav-194">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> and that "if Stepinac had responded to the charges against him, his defense would have inevitably unraveled, exposing the Vatican's support of the <a href="/wiki/Genocide" title="Genocide">genocidal</a> Pavelić".<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> Throughout 1942, the <a href="/wiki/Yugoslav_government_in_exile" class="mw-redirect" title="Yugoslav government in exile">Yugoslav government in exile</a> sent letters of protest to Pius XII asking him to use all possible means to stop the massacres against the <a href="/wiki/Persecution_of_Serbs_in_the_Independent_State_of_Croatia" class="mw-redirect" title="Persecution of Serbs in the Independent State of Croatia">Serbs</a> in the <a href="/wiki/NDH" class="mw-redirect" title="NDH">NDH</a>, however Pius XII did nothing.<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1941, Pius XII interpreted <i><a href="/wiki/Divini_Redemptoris" title="Divini Redemptoris">Divini Redemptoris</a></i>, an <a href="/wiki/Encyclical" title="Encyclical">encyclical</a> of Pope Pius XI, which forbade Catholics to help Communists, as not applying to military assistance to the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>. This interpretation assuaged American Catholics who had previously opposed <a href="/wiki/Lend-Lease" title="Lend-Lease">Lend-Lease</a> arrangements with the Soviet Union.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In March 1942, Pius XII established diplomatic relations with the <a href="/wiki/Empire_of_Japan" title="Empire of Japan">Japanese Empire</a> and received ambassador <a href="/wiki/Ken_Harada_(diplomat)" title="Ken Harada (diplomat)">Ken Harada</a>, who remained in that position until the end of the war.<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In June 1942, diplomatic relations were established with the <a href="/wiki/Nationalist_government" title="Nationalist government">Nationalist government</a> of China. This step was envisaged earlier, but delayed due to Japanese pressure to establish relations with the pro-Japanese Wang Jingwei government. The first <a href="/wiki/Embassy_of_the_Republic_of_China_to_the_Holy_See" class="mw-redirect" title="Embassy of the Republic of China to the Holy See">Chinese Minister to the Vatican</a>, Hsieh Shou-kang, was only able to arrive at the Vatican in January 1943, due to difficulties of travel resulting from the war. He remained in that position until late 1946.<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Pope employed the new technology of <a href="/wiki/Radio" title="Radio">radio</a> and a series of Christmas messages to preach against selfish nationalism and the evils of modern warfare and offer sympathy to the victims of the war.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_186-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-186">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII%27s_1942_Christmas_address" title="Pope Pius XII&#39;s 1942 Christmas address">Pius XII's 1942 Christmas address</a> via <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Radio" title="Vatican Radio">Vatican Radio</a> voiced concern at <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_abuses" class="mw-redirect" title="Human rights abuses">human rights abuses</a> and the murder of innocents based on race. The majority of the speech spoke generally about human rights and civil society; at the very end of the speech, Pius XII mentioned "the hundreds of thousands of persons who, without any fault on their part, sometimes only because of their nationality or race, have been consigned to death or to a slow decline".<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> According to Rittner, the speech remains a "lightning rod" in debates about Pius XII.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> The Nazis themselves responded to the speech by stating that it was "one long attack on everything we stand for.&#160;... He is clearly speaking on behalf of the Jews.&#160;... He is virtually accusing the German people of injustice toward the Jews, and makes himself the mouthpiece of the Jewish war criminals." The New York Times wrote that "The voice of Pius XII is a lonely voice in the silence and darkness enveloping Europe this Christmas.&#160;... In calling for a 'real new order' based on 'liberty, justice and love',&#160;... the pope put himself squarely against Hitlerism."<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> Historian Michael Phayer claims, however, that "it is still not clear <i>whose</i> genocide or <i>which</i> genocide he was referring to".<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> Speaking on the 50th anniversary of Pius's death in 2008, the German Pope <a href="/wiki/Benedict_XVI" class="mw-redirect" title="Benedict XVI">Benedict XVI</a> recalled that the Pope's voice had been "broken by emotion" as he "deplored the situation" with a "clear reference to the deportation and extermination of the Jews".<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Several authors have <a href="/wiki/Alleged_plot_to_kidnap_Pope_Pius_XII" title="Alleged plot to kidnap Pope Pius XII">alleged a plot to kidnap Pius XII</a> by the Nazis during their occupation of Rome in 1943 (Vatican City itself was not occupied); British historian <a href="/wiki/Owen_Chadwick" title="Owen Chadwick">Owen Chadwick</a> and the Jesuit <a href="/wiki/Actes_et_documents_du_Saint_Si%C3%A8ge_relatifs_%C3%A0_la_Seconde_Guerre_Mondiale" title="Actes et documents du Saint Siège relatifs à la Seconde Guerre Mondiale">ADSS</a> editor <a href="/wiki/Robert_A._Graham" title="Robert A. Graham">Rev. Robert Graham</a> each concluded such claims were an intentional creation of the British <a href="/wiki/Political_Warfare_Executive" title="Political Warfare Executive">Political Warfare Executive</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup> However, in 2007, subsequent to those accounts, Dan Kurzman published a work which he maintains establishes that the plot was a fact.<sup id="cite_ref-kix_207-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kix-207">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1944, Pius XII issued a Christmas message in which he warned against rule by the masses and against secular conceptions of liberty and equality.<sup id="cite_ref-Pius1944_135-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pius1944-135">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Final_stages">Final stages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Final stages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>As the war was approaching its end in 1945, Pius advocated a lenient policy by the <a href="/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II" title="Allies of World War II">Allied</a> leaders in an effort to prevent what he perceived to be the mistakes made at the end of World War I.<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup> On 23 August 1944, he met British Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Winston_Churchill" title="Winston Churchill">Winston Churchill</a>, who was visiting Rome. At their meeting, the Pope acknowledged the justice of punishing war criminals, but expressed a hope that the people of Italy would not be punished, preferring that they be made "full allies" in the remaining war effort.<sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Holocaust">Holocaust <span class="anchor" id="The_Holocaust"></span></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Holocaust">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII_and_the_Holocaust" title="Pope Pius XII and the Holocaust">Pope Pius XII and the Holocaust</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII_and_Judaism" title="Pope Pius XII and Judaism">Pope Pius XII and Judaism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII_and_the_Roman_razzia" class="mw-redirect" title="Pope Pius XII and the Roman razzia">Pope Pius XII and the Roman razzia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pius_Wars" title="Pius Wars">Pius Wars</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-H26878,_Berlin,_Neujahrsempfang_in_der_neuen_Reichskanzlei.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-H26878%2C_Berlin%2C_Neujahrsempfang_in_der_neuen_Reichskanzlei.jpg/220px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-H26878%2C_Berlin%2C_Neujahrsempfang_in_der_neuen_Reichskanzlei.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-H26878%2C_Berlin%2C_Neujahrsempfang_in_der_neuen_Reichskanzlei.jpg/330px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-H26878%2C_Berlin%2C_Neujahrsempfang_in_der_neuen_Reichskanzlei.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-H26878%2C_Berlin%2C_Neujahrsempfang_in_der_neuen_Reichskanzlei.jpg/440px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-H26878%2C_Berlin%2C_Neujahrsempfang_in_der_neuen_Reichskanzlei.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="546" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Cesare_Orsenigo" title="Cesare Orsenigo">Cesare Orsenigo</a>, Pius XII's nuncio to Germany throughout World War II, with Hitler and <a href="/wiki/Joachim_von_Ribbentrop" title="Joachim von Ribbentrop">Joachim von Ribbentrop</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Toasting_Polish_Dachau.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Toasting_Polish_Dachau.jpg/220px-Toasting_Polish_Dachau.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Toasting_Polish_Dachau.jpg/330px-Toasting_Polish_Dachau.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Toasting_Polish_Dachau.jpg/440px-Toasting_Polish_Dachau.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2709" data-file-height="2008" /></a><figcaption>Polish prisoners toast their liberation from <a href="/wiki/Dachau_Concentration_Camp" class="mw-redirect" title="Dachau Concentration Camp">Dachau</a>. Nazi persecution of Catholics was at its <a href="/wiki/Nazi_persecution_of_the_Catholic_Church_in_Poland" title="Nazi persecution of the Catholic Church in Poland">most severe</a> in <a href="/wiki/Occupied_Poland" class="mw-redirect" title="Occupied Poland">occupied Poland</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Pius_XII_by_Peter_McIntyre_(10044850276).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Pope_Pius_XII_by_Peter_McIntyre_%2810044850276%29.jpg/220px-Pope_Pius_XII_by_Peter_McIntyre_%2810044850276%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="268" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Pope_Pius_XII_by_Peter_McIntyre_%2810044850276%29.jpg/330px-Pope_Pius_XII_by_Peter_McIntyre_%2810044850276%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Pope_Pius_XII_by_Peter_McIntyre_%2810044850276%29.jpg/440px-Pope_Pius_XII_by_Peter_McIntyre_%2810044850276%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="577" data-file-height="703" /></a><figcaption>Pope Pius XII by <a href="/wiki/Peter_McIntyre_(artist)" title="Peter McIntyre (artist)">Peter McIntyre</a> c.1943–1944</figcaption></figure>
