<p>During the Second World War, after Nazi Germany commenced its mass-murder of Jews in occupied Soviet territory, Pius XII employed diplomacy to aid victims of the Holocaust and directed the church to provide discreet aid to Jews.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_210-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-210">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup> Upon his death in 1958, among many Jewish tributes, the Chief Rabbi of Rome <a href="/wiki/Elio_Toaff" title="Elio Toaff">Elio Toaff</a>, said: "Jews will always remember what the Catholic Church did for them by order of the Pope during the Second World War. When the war was raging, Pius spoke out very often to condemn the false race theory."<sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> This is disputed by commentator John Cornwell, who in his book, <i><a href="/wiki/Hitler%27s_Pope" title="Hitler&#39;s Pope">Hitler's Pope</a></i>, argues that the pope was weak and vacillating in his approach to Nazism. Cornwell asserts that the pope did little to challenge the progressing holocaust of the Jews out of fear of provoking the Nazis into invading Vatican City.<sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In his 1939 <i><a href="/wiki/Summi_Pontificatus" title="Summi Pontificatus">Summi Pontificatus</a></i> first papal encyclical, Pius reiterated Catholic teaching against racial persecution and antisemitism and affirmed the ethical principles of the "<a href="/wiki/Ten_Commandments" title="Ten Commandments">Revelation on Sinai</a>". At Christmas 1942, once evidence of the mass-murder of Jews had emerged, Pius XII <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII%27s_1942_Christmas_address" title="Pope Pius XII&#39;s 1942 Christmas address">voiced concern</a> at the murder of "hundreds of thousands" of "faultless" people because of their "nationality or race" and intervened to attempt to block Nazi deportations of Jews in various countries. Upon his death in 1958, Pius was praised emphatically by the Israeli Foreign Minister, and other world leaders. But his insistence on Vatican neutrality and avoidance of naming the Nazis as the evildoers of the conflict became the foundation for contemporary and later criticisms from some quarters. His strongest public condemnation of genocide was considered inadequate by the Allied Powers, while the Nazis viewed him as an Allied sympathizer who had dishonored his policy of Vatican neutrality.<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup> Hitler biographer John Toland, while scathing of Pius's cautious public comments in relation to the mistreatment of Jews, concluded that the Allies' own record of action against the Holocaust was "shameful", while "The Church, under the Pope's guidance, had already saved the lives of more Jews than all other churches, religious institutions and rescue organizations combined".<sup id="cite_ref-John_Toland_p.760_179-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-John_Toland_p.760-179">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1939, the newly elected Pope Pius XII appointed several prominent Jewish scholars to posts at the Vatican after they had been dismissed from Italian universities under <a href="/wiki/Italian_Fascism" class="mw-redirect" title="Italian Fascism">Fascist</a> leader <a href="/wiki/Benito_Mussolini" title="Benito Mussolini">Benito Mussolini</a>'s racial laws.<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> In 1939, the Pope employed a Jewish cartographer, Roberto Almagia, to work on old maps in the Vatican library. Almagia had been at the <a href="/wiki/Sapienza_University_of_Rome" title="Sapienza University of Rome">University of Rome</a> since 1915 but was dismissed after <a href="/wiki/Benito_Mussolini" title="Benito Mussolini">Benito Mussolini</a>'s antisemitic legislation of 1938. The Pope's appointment of two Jews to the Vatican Academy of Science as well as the hiring of Almagia were reported by <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_York_Times" title="The New York Times">The New York Times</a></i> in the editions of 11 November 1939 and 10 January 1940.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius later engineered an agreement — formally approved on 23 June 1939 — with Brazilian <a href="/wiki/President_of_Brazil" title="President of Brazil">President</a> <a href="/wiki/Get%C3%BAlio_Vargas" title="Getúlio Vargas">Getúlio Vargas</a> to issue 3,000 <a href="/wiki/Visa_(document)" class="mw-redirect" title="Visa (document)">visas</a> to "non-<a href="/wiki/Aryan" title="Aryan">Aryan</a> Catholics". However, over the next 18 months Brazil's Conselho de Imigração e Colonização (CIC) continued to tighten the restrictions on their issuance, including requiring a <a href="/wiki/Baptism" title="Baptism">baptismal certificate</a> dated before 1933, a substantial monetary transfer to the <a href="/wiki/Banco_do_Brasil" title="Banco do Brasil">Banco do Brasil</a>, and approval by the Brazilian Propaganda Office in Berlin.<sup id="cite_ref-30giorni.it_216-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30giorni.it-216">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> The program was cancelled 14 months later, after fewer than 1,000 visas had been issued, amid suspicions of "improper conduct" (i.e., continuing to practice Judaism) among those who had received visas.<sup id="cite_ref-gutman1136_58-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gutman1136-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-217" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-217">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In April 1939, after the submission of <a href="/wiki/Charles_Maurras" title="Charles Maurras">Charles Maurras</a> and the intervention of the Carmel of <a href="/wiki/Lisieux" title="Lisieux">Lisieux</a>, Pius XII ended his predecessor's ban on <a href="/wiki/Action_Fran%C3%A7aise" title="Action Française">Action Française</a>, a virulently <a href="/wiki/Antisemitic" class="mw-redirect" title="Antisemitic">antisemitic</a> organization.<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-McInerny-p49_219-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-McInerny-p49-219">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following the German/Soviet invasion of Poland, the Pope's first encyclical, <i>Summi Pontificatus</i> reiterated Catholic teaching against racial persecution and rejected antisemitism, quoting scripture singling out the "principle of equality" — with specific reference to Jews: "there is neither Gentile nor Jew, circumcision nor uncircumcision" and direct affirmation of the Jewish <i>Revelation on Sinai</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup> The forgetting of solidarity "imposed by our common origin and by the equality of rational nature in all men" was called "pernicious error".<sup id="cite_ref-Pius_XII_161-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pius_XII-161">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Catholics everywhere were called upon to offer "compassion and help" to the victims of the war.<sup id="cite_ref-http_162-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-http-162">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> The Pope declared determination to work to hasten the return of peace and trust in prayers for justice, love and mercy, to prevail against the scourge of war.<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> The letter also decried the deaths of noncombatants.<sup id="cite_ref-Pius_XII_1939_164-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pius_XII_1939-164">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a> Luigi Maglione received a request from <a href="/wiki/Chief_Rabbi" title="Chief Rabbi">Chief Rabbi</a> of <a href="/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine" title="Mandatory Palestine">Palestine</a> <a href="/wiki/Yitzhak_HaLevi_Herzog" title="Yitzhak HaLevi Herzog">Isaac Herzog</a> in the spring of 1940 to intercede on behalf of <a href="/wiki/Lithuania" title="Lithuania">Lithuanian</a> Jews about to be deported to Germany.<sup id="cite_ref-gutman1136_58-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gutman1136-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Pius called <a href="/wiki/Joachim_von_Ribbentrop" title="Joachim von Ribbentrop">Ribbentrop</a> on 11 March, repeatedly protesting against the treatment of Jews.<sup id="cite_ref-McInerny-p49_219-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-McInerny-p49-219">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> In 1940, Pius asked members of the clergy, on Vatican letterhead, to do whatever they could on behalf of interned Jews.<sup id="cite_ref-us081117_223-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-us081117-223">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1941, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Theodor_Innitzer" title="Theodor Innitzer">Theodor Innitzer</a> of <a href="/wiki/Vienna" title="Vienna">Vienna</a> informed Pius of Jewish deportations in Vienna.<sup id="cite_ref-gutman1137_224-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gutman1137-224">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> Later that year, when asked by French Marshal <a href="/wiki/Philippe_P%C3%A9tain" title="Philippe Pétain">Philippe Pétain</a> if the Vatican objected to antisemitic laws, Pius responded that the church condemned antisemitism, but would not comment on specific rules.<sup id="cite_ref-gutman1137_224-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gutman1137-224">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> Similarly, when <a href="/wiki/Philippe_P%C3%A9tain" title="Philippe Pétain">Philippe Pétain</a>'s regime adopted the "Jewish statutes", the <a href="/wiki/Vichy_France" title="Vichy France">Vichy</a> ambassador to the Vatican, <a href="/wiki/L%C3%A9on_B%C3%A9rard" title="Léon Bérard">Léon Bérard</a> (a French politician), was told that the legislation did not conflict with Catholic teachings.<sup id="cite_ref-Perl-p200_225-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Perl-p200-225">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Valerio_Valeri" title="Valerio Valeri">Valerio Valeri</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Nuncio" title="Nuncio">nuncio</a> to France, was "embarrassed" when he learned of this publicly from Pétain<sup id="cite_ref-Phayer,_2000,_p._5_226-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Phayer,_2000,_p._5-226">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> and personally checked the information with Cardinal Secretary of State Maglione<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup> who confirmed the Vatican's position.<sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> In June 1942, Pius XII personally protested against the mass deportations of Jews from France, ordering the papal nuncio to protest to Pétain against "the inhuman arrests and deportations of Jews".<sup id="cite_ref-229" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-229">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup> In September 1941, Pius XII objected to a <a href="/wiki/Slovaks" title="Slovaks">Slovak</a> Jewish Code,<sup id="cite_ref-230" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-230">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup> which, unlike the earlier Vichy codes, prohibited intermarriage between Jews and non-Jews.<sup id="cite_ref-Phayer,_2000,_p._5_226-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Phayer,_2000,_p._5-226">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> In October 1941, <a href="/wiki/Harold_H._Tittmann_Jr." title="Harold H. Tittmann Jr.">Harold Tittmann</a>, a US delegate to the Vatican, asked the pope to condemn the atrocities against Jews; Pius replied that the Vatican wished to remain "neutral",<sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> reiterating the neutrality policy which Pius invoked as early as September 1940.<sup id="cite_ref-Perl-p200_225-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Perl-p200-225">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1942, the Slovak <a href="/wiki/Charg%C3%A9_d%27affaires" title="Chargé d&#39;affaires">chargé d'affaires</a> told Pius that <a href="/wiki/Slovaks" title="Slovaks">Slovak</a> Jews were being sent to concentration camps.<sup id="cite_ref-gutman1137_224-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gutman1137-224">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> On 11 March 1942, several days before the first transport was due to leave, the chargé d'affaires in <a href="/wiki/Bratislava" title="Bratislava">Bratislava</a> reported to the Vatican: "I have been assured that this atrocious plan is the handwork of ... Prime Minister (<a href="/wiki/Vojtech_Tuka" title="Vojtech Tuka">Tuka</a>), who confirmed the plan ... he dared to tell me — he who makes such a show of his Catholicism — that he saw nothing inhuman or un-Christian in it ... the deportation of 80,000 persons to Poland, is equivalent to condemning a great number of them to certain death." The Vatican protested to the Slovak government that it "deplore(s) these... measures which gravely hurt the natural human rights of persons, merely because of their race."<sup id="cite_ref-232" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-232">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 18 September 1942, Pius XII received a letter from Monsignor Montini (future <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a>), saying "the massacres of the Jews reach frightening proportions and forms".<sup id="cite_ref-gutman1137_224-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gutman1137-224">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> Later that month, <a href="/wiki/Myron_Taylor" class="mw-redirect" title="Myron Taylor">Myron Taylor</a>, U.S. representative to the Vatican, warned Pius that the Vatican's "moral prestige" was being injured by silence on European atrocities, a warning which was echoed simultaneously by representatives from the United Kingdom, Brazil, <a href="/wiki/Uruguay" title="Uruguay">Uruguay</a>, Belgium, and Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-233" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-233">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup> Myron C. Taylor passed a US Government memorandum to Pius on 26 September 1942, outlining intelligence received from the Jewish Agency for Palestine which said that Jews from across the <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Empire" class="mw-redirect" title="Nazi Empire">Nazi Empire</a> were being systematically "butchered". Taylor asked if the Vatican might have any information which might "tend to confirm the reports", and if so, what the Pope might be able to do to influence public opinion against the "barbarities".<sup id="cite_ref-234" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-234">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> Cardinal Maglione handed Harold Tittmann a response to the letter on 10 October. The note thanked Washington for passing on the intelligence, and confirmed that reports of severe measures against the Jews had reached the Vatican from other sources, though it had not been possible to "verify their accuracy". Nevertheless, "every opportunity is being taken by the Holy See, however, to mitigate the suffering of these unfortunate people".<sup id="cite_ref-235" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-235">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup> In December 1942, when Tittmann asked Cardinal Secretary of State Maglione if Pius would issue a proclamation similar to the Allied declaration "German Policy of Extermination of the Jewish Race", Maglione replied that the Vatican was "unable to denounce publicly particular atrocities".<sup id="cite_ref-236" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-236">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> Pius XII directly explained to Tittman that he could not name the Nazis without at the same time mentioning the Bolsheviks.<sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following the Nazi/Soviet invasion of Poland, Pius XII's <i><a href="/wiki/Summi_Pontificatus" title="Summi Pontificatus">Summi Pontificatus</a></i> called for the sympathy of the whole world towards Poland, where "the blood of countless human beings, even noncombatants" was being spilled.<sup id="cite_ref-Pius_XII_1939_164-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pius_XII_1939-164">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> Pius never publicly condemned the Nazi massacre of 1,800,000–1,900,000 Poles, overwhelmingly Catholic (including 2,935 members of the Catholic clergy).<sup id="cite_ref-238" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-238">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-239" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-239">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup> In late 1942, Pius XII advised German and Hungarian bishops to speak out against the massacres on the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Front_(World_War_II)" title="Eastern Front (World War II)">Eastern Front</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-240" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-240">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup> In his 1942 Christmas Eve message, he expressed concern for "those hundreds of thousands, who ... sometimes only by reason of their nationality or race, are marked down for death or progressive extinction.<sup id="cite_ref-241" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-241">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> On 7 April 1943, Msgr. Tardini, one of Pius XII's closest advisors, advised Pius XII that it would be politically advantageous after the war to take steps to help Slovak Jews.<sup id="cite_ref-242" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-242">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In January 1943, Pius XII declined to denounce publicly the Nazi discrimination against the Jews, following requests to do so from <a href="/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_Raczkiewicz" title="Władysław Raczkiewicz">Władysław Raczkiewicz</a>, president of the Polish <a href="/wiki/Government-in-exile" class="mw-redirect" title="Government-in-exile">government-in-exile</a>, and Bishop <a href="/wiki/Konrad_von_Preysing" title="Konrad von Preysing">Konrad von Preysing</a> of Berlin.<sup id="cite_ref-243" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-243">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup> According to Toland, in June 1943, Pius XII addressed the issue of mistreatment of Jews at a conference of the <a href="/wiki/Sacred_College_of_Cardinals" class="mw-redirect" title="Sacred College of Cardinals">Sacred College of Cardinals</a> and said: "Every word We address to the competent authority on this subject, and all Our public utterances have to be carefully weighed and measured by Us in the interests of the victims themselves, lest, contrary to Our intentions, We make their situation worse and harder to bear".<sup id="cite_ref-John_Toland_p.760_179-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-John_Toland_p.760-179">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 26 September 1943, following the German occupation of northern Italy, Nazi officials gave Jewish leaders in Rome 36 hours to produce 50 kilograms (110&#160;lb) of gold (or the equivalent) threatening to take 300 hostages. Then Chief Rabbi of Rome <a href="/wiki/Israel_Zolli" title="Israel Zolli">Israel Zolli</a> recounts in his memoir that he was selected to go to the Vatican and seek help.<sup id="cite_ref-244" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-244">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup> The Vatican offered to loan 15 kilos, but the offer proved unnecessary when the Jews received an extension.<sup id="cite_ref-245" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-245">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup> Soon afterward, when deportations from Italy were imminent, 477 Jews were hidden in the Vatican itself and another 4,238 were protected in Roman monasteries and convents.<sup id="cite_ref-246" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-246">&#91;245&#93;</a></sup> Eighty percent of Roman Jews were saved from deportation.<sup id="cite_ref-247" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-247">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup> Phayer argues that the German diplomats in Rome were the "initiators of the effort to save the city's Jews", but holds that Pius XII "cooperated in this attempt at rescue", while agreeing with Zuccotti that the pope "did not give orders" for any Catholic institution to hide Jews.<sup id="cite_ref-248" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-248">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 30 April 1943, Pius XII wrote to Bishop <a href="/wiki/Konrad_von_Preysing" title="Konrad von Preysing">Graf von Preysing</a> of Berlin to say: "We give to the pastors who are working on the local level the duty of determining if and to what degree the danger of reprisals and of various forms of oppression occasioned by episcopal declarations&#160;... <i>ad maiora mala vitanda</i> (to avoid worse)&#160;... seem to advise caution. Here lies one of the reasons, why We impose self-restraint on Ourselves in our speeches; the experience, that we made in 1942 with papal addresses, which We authorized to be forwarded to the Believers, justifies our opinion, as far as We see.&#160;... The Holy See has done whatever was in its power, with charitable, financial and moral assistance. To say nothing of the substantial sums which we spent in American money for the fares of immigrants."<sup id="cite_ref-249" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-249">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 28 October 1943, <a href="/wiki/Ernst_von_Weizs%C3%A4cker" title="Ernst von Weizsäcker">Ernst von Weizsäcker</a>, the German Ambassador to the Vatican, telegraphed Berlin that "the Pope has not yet let himself be persuaded to make an official condemnation of the deportation of the Roman Jews.&#160;... Since it is currently thought that the Germans will take no further steps against the Jews in Rome, the question of our relations with the Vatican may be considered closed."<sup id="cite_ref-250" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-250">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-251" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-251">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In March 1944, through the papal nuncio in <a href="/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest">Budapest</a>, Angelo Rotta, the pope urged the <a href="/wiki/Hungary" title="Hungary">Hungarian</a> government to moderate its treatment of the Jews.<sup id="cite_ref-gutman1138_252-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gutman1138-252">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup> The pope ordered Rotta and other papal legates to hide and shelter Jews.<sup id="cite_ref-253" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-253">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup> These protests, along with others from the King of Sweden, the International Red Cross, the United States, and Britain led to the cessation of deportations on 8 July 1944.<sup id="cite_ref-254" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-254">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup> Also in 1944, Pius appealed to 13 Latin American governments to accept "emergency passports", although it also took the intervention of the U.S. State Department for those countries to honor the documents.<sup id="cite_ref-255" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-255">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Ernst_Kaltenbrunner" title="Ernst Kaltenbrunner">Kaltenbrunner</a> Report to Hitler, dated 29 November 1944, against the backdrop of the <a href="/wiki/20_July_plot" title="20 July plot">20 July 1944 Plot to assassinate Hitler</a>, states that the Pope was somehow a conspirator, specifically naming Eugenio Pacelli (Pope Pius XII), as being a party in the attempt.<sup id="cite_ref-256" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-256">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Jewish_orphans_controversy">Jewish orphans controversy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Jewish orphans controversy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Jewish_orphans_controversy" title="Jewish orphans controversy">Jewish orphans controversy</a></div>
<p>In 2005, <i><a href="/wiki/Corriere_della_Sera" title="Corriere della Sera">Corriere della Sera</a></i> published a document dated 20 November 1946 on the subject of Jewish children baptized in war-time France. The document ordered that baptized children, if orphaned, should be kept in Catholic custody and stated that the decision "has been approved by the Holy Father". Nuncio <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">Angelo Roncalli</a> (who became Pope John XXIII, and was recognized by <a href="/wiki/Yad_Vashem" title="Yad Vashem">Yad Vashem</a> as <a href="/wiki/Righteous_Among_the_Nations" title="Righteous Among the Nations">Righteous Among the Nations</a>) ignored this directive.<sup id="cite_ref-257" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-257">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Abe_Foxman" class="mw-redirect" title="Abe Foxman">Abe Foxman</a>, the national director of the <a href="/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League" title="Anti-Defamation League">Anti-Defamation League</a> (ADL), who had himself been baptized as a child and had undergone a custody battle afterwards, called for an immediate freeze on Pius's beatification process until the relevant <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Secret_Archives" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican Secret Archives">Vatican Secret Archives</a> and baptismal records were opened.<sup id="cite_ref-258" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-258">&#91;257&#93;</a></sup> Two Italian scholars, Matteo Luigi Napolitano and Andrea Tornielli, confirmed that the memorandum was genuine although the reporting by the <i>Corriere della Sera</i> was misleading, as the document had originated in the French Catholic Church archives rather than the Vatican archives and strictly concerned itself with children without living blood relatives who were supposed to be handed over to Jewish organizations.<sup id="cite_ref-259" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-259">&#91;258&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Writings from released Vatican records revealed that Pius XII was personally but secretly involved in hiding the <a href="/wiki/Finaly_Affair" title="Finaly Affair">Finaly children</a> from their Jewish family in an ultimately failed attempt to keep them Catholic after their secret baptism done against the wishes of their family. The French Catholic Church received very bad press from the affair, and several nuns and monks were jailed for the kidnapping before the children were discovered and spirited away to Israel. But it is only recently that the Pope's personal involvement was revealed.<sup id="cite_ref-260" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-260">&#91;259&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-World_War_II">Post-World War II</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Post-World War II">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII_foreign_relations_after_World_War_II" title="Pope Pius XII foreign relations after World War II">Pope Pius XII foreign relations after World War II</a>, <a href="/wiki/Persecutions_of_the_Catholic_Church_and_Pius_XII" title="Persecutions of the Catholic Church and Pius XII">Persecutions of the Catholic Church and Pius XII</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII_and_Russia" title="Pope Pius XII and Russia">Pope Pius XII and Russia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII_and_China" title="Pope Pius XII and China">Pope Pius XII and China</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII_and_Poland" title="Pope Pius XII and Poland">Pope Pius XII and Poland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Catholic_clergy_involvement_with_the_Usta%C5%A1e" title="Catholic clergy involvement with the Ustaše">Catholic clergy involvement with the Ustaše</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Ratlines_(World_War_II_aftermath)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ratlines (World War II aftermath)">Ratlines (World War II aftermath)</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bundesarchiv_B_145_Bild-F007416-0008,_Bahnhof_Bonn,_Verabschiedung_Apostolischer_Nuntius.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Bundesarchiv_B_145_Bild-F007416-0008%2C_Bahnhof_Bonn%2C_Verabschiedung_Apostolischer_Nuntius.jpg/220px-Bundesarchiv_B_145_Bild-F007416-0008%2C_Bahnhof_Bonn%2C_Verabschiedung_Apostolischer_Nuntius.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="229" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Bundesarchiv_B_145_Bild-F007416-0008%2C_Bahnhof_Bonn%2C_Verabschiedung_Apostolischer_Nuntius.jpg/330px-Bundesarchiv_B_145_Bild-F007416-0008%2C_Bahnhof_Bonn%2C_Verabschiedung_Apostolischer_Nuntius.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Bundesarchiv_B_145_Bild-F007416-0008%2C_Bahnhof_Bonn%2C_Verabschiedung_Apostolischer_Nuntius.jpg/440px-Bundesarchiv_B_145_Bild-F007416-0008%2C_Bahnhof_Bonn%2C_Verabschiedung_Apostolischer_Nuntius.jpg 2x" data-file-width="762" data-file-height="794" /></a><figcaption>Bishop <a href="/wiki/Aloisius_Joseph_Muench" title="Aloisius Joseph Muench">Aloisius Joseph Muench</a>, Pius XII's post-war liaison to the <a href="/wiki/Office_of_Military_Government,_United_States" title="Office of Military Government, United States">Office of Military Government, United States</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>After World War II, Pope Pius XII focused on material aid to war-torn Europe, an internal internationalization of the Catholic Church, and the development of its worldwide diplomatic relations. His encyclicals, <i><a href="/wiki/Evangelii_praecones" title="Evangelii praecones">Evangelii praecones</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Fidei_donum" class="mw-redirect" title="Fidei donum">Fidei donum</a></i>, issued on 2 June 1951 and 21 April 1957, respectively, increased the local decision-making of Catholic missions, many of which became independent dioceses. Pius XII demanded recognition of local cultures as fully equal to European culture.<sup id="cite_ref-261" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-261">&#91;260&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-262" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-262">&#91;261&#93;</a></sup> Though his language retained old conceptions&#160;– Africa, for example, merited special attention since the church there worked 'to forward her work among the heathen multitudes' – in 1956 he expressed solidarity with the 'non-Europeans who aspire to full political independence'.<sup id="cite_ref-263" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-263">&#91;262&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the immediate aftermath of the war, Pius XII <a href="/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_Pope_Pius_XII_in_1946" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinals created by Pope Pius XII in 1946">elevated a number</a> of high-profile resistors of Nazism to the College of Cardinals in 1946, among them the German Bishops <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Frings" class="mw-redirect" title="Joseph Frings">Joseph Frings</a> of Cologne, <a href="/wiki/Clemens_August_Graf_von_Galen" title="Clemens August Graf von Galen">Clemens von Galen</a> of Münster and <a href="/wiki/Konrad_von_Preysing" title="Konrad von Preysing">Konrad von Preysing</a> of Berlin. From elsewhere in the liberated <a href="/wiki/Greater_Germanic_Reich" title="Greater Germanic Reich">Nazi Empire</a> Pius selected other resistors: Dutch Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Johannes_de_Jong" title="Johannes de Jong">Johannes de Jong</a>; Hungarian Bishop <a href="/wiki/J%C3%B3zsef_Mindszenty" title="József Mindszenty">József Mindszenty</a>; Polish Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Adam_Stefan_Sapieha" title="Adam Stefan Sapieha">Adam Stefan Sapieha</a>; and French Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Jules-G%C3%A9raud_Sali%C3%A8ge" title="Jules-Géraud Saliège">Jules-Géraud Saliège</a>. In 1946 and 1953, respectively, he named as cardinals <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Tien_Ken-sin" title="Thomas Tien Ken-sin">Thomas Tien</a> of China and <a href="/wiki/Valerian_Gracias" title="Valerian Gracias">Valerian Gracias</a> of India&#160;– the first indigenous Catholics of their respective nations to sit in the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-264" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-264">&#91;263&#93;</a></sup> Italian Papal diplomat <a href="/wiki/Angelo_Roncalli" class="mw-redirect" title="Angelo Roncalli">Angelo Roncalli</a> (later Pope John XXIII) and Polish Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Stefan_Wyszy%C5%84ski" title="Stefan Wyszyński">Stefan Wyszyński</a> were others among those <a href="/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_Pope_Pius_XII_in_1953" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinals created by Pope Pius XII in 1953">elevated in 1953</a>.
</p><p>A German contingent dominated his inner circle at this period&#160;– the German Jesuits <a href="/wiki/Robert_Leiber" title="Robert Leiber">Robert Leiber</a>, Wilhelm Hentrich and Ivo Zeiger. His personal confessor Augustin Bea was a German Jesuit and Mother Pascalina Lehnert and the other German speaking sisters of the papal household added to this element. The American bishop <a href="/wiki/Aloisius_Muench" class="mw-redirect" title="Aloisius Muench">Aloisius Muench</a> wrote in November 1948 that Pius XII was 'more interested in affairs of the Church in Germany than in any other part of the Church' and resolved to make the postwar German crisis a top priority – 'its refugee crisis, poverty, hunger and disease, the fate of prisoners-of-war and accused war criminals, the disruption to the internal organization and communal life of German Catholicism, and Germany's uncertain political future'.<sup id="cite_ref-265" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-265">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He was concerned too about the potential spread of Communism in Western Europe and the Americas. As he sought to secure resources from abroad to aid post-war recovery, believing deprivation fuelled political agitation, so he also sought to influence Italian politics. In January 1948, Luigi Gedda, of Italy's <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Action" title="Catholic Action">Catholic Action</a> movement, was called to the Vatican as the campaign for the first parliament of Italy's post-fascist republic was underway.<sup id="cite_ref-266" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-266">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius XII was rather distrustful of <a href="/wiki/Alcide_de_Gasperi" class="mw-redirect" title="Alcide de Gasperi">Alcide de Gasperi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Christian_Democracy_(Italy)" title="Christian Democracy (Italy)">Italy's Christian Democrats</a>, considering the party indecisive and fractious&#160;– reformist currents within it particularly, which tended to the moderate Left&#160;– represented by the Sicilian priest <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Sturzo" title="Luigi Sturzo">Luigi Sturzo</a> for example&#160;– he considered too accommodating to the Left. On the eve of the 1952 local elections in Rome, in which again the Communist and Socialist parties threatened to win out, he used informal connections to make his views known. Pius XII stated that the war against Communism was a holy war and excommunicated members of the Communist Party. Having decided to encourage the Christian Democrats to consider a political alliance with the Rightist parties as part of an anti-left coalition, he asked the Jesuit, Father Riccardo Lombardi, to speak with de Gasperi to consider such an alliance&#160;– an electoral alliance with those even of monarchist and neo-fascist tendencies -including the <a href="/wiki/Italian_Social_Movement" title="Italian Social Movement">Italian Social Movement</a>. Adopting a domino theory he warned that, if "the Communists win in Rome, in Italy, it will cast a shadow on the entire world: France would become Communist, and then Spain and then all of Europe."<sup id="cite_ref-267" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-267">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Later_life.2C_illness_and_death"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Later_life,_illness_and_death">Later life, illness and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Later life, illness and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Late_years_of_Pope_Pius_XII">Late years of Pope Pius XII</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Late years of Pope Pius XII">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Late_years_of_Pope_Pius_XII" title="Late years of Pope Pius XII">Late years of Pope Pius XII</a></div>
<p>A long illness in late 1954 caused the Pope to consider <a href="/wiki/Abdication" title="Abdication">abdication</a>. Afterwards, changes in his work habit became noticeable. The Pope avoided long ceremonies, canonizations and consistories and displayed hesitancy in personnel matters. He found it increasingly difficult to chastise subordinates and appointees such as his physician, <a href="/wiki/Riccardo_Galeazzi-Lisi" title="Riccardo Galeazzi-Lisi">Riccardo Galeazzi-Lisi</a>, who after numerous indiscretions was excluded from Papal service for the last years, but, keeping his title, was able to enter the papal apartments to make photos of the dying Pope, which he sold to French magazines.<sup id="cite_ref-268" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-268">&#91;267&#93;</a></sup> Pius underwent three courses of <a href="/wiki/Rejuvenation_(aging)" class="mw-redirect" title="Rejuvenation (aging)">cellular rejuvenation</a> treatment administered by <a href="/wiki/Paul_Niehans" title="Paul Niehans">Paul Niehans</a>, the most important in 1954 when Pius was gravely ill. Side-effects of the treatment included hallucinations, from which the Pope suffered in his last years. "These years were also plagued by horrific nightmares. Pacelli's blood-curdling screams could be heard throughout the papal apartments."<sup id="cite_ref-269" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-269">&#91;268&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius XII often elevated young priests as bishops, such as <a href="/wiki/Julius_D%C3%B6pfner" title="Julius Döpfner">Julius Döpfner</a> (35 years) and Karol Wojtyła (later <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>, 38 years), one of his last appointees in 1958. He took a firm stand against pastoral experiments, such as "<a href="/wiki/Worker-priest" title="Worker-priest">worker-priests</a>", who worked full-time in factories and joined political parties and unions. He continued to defend the theological tradition of <a href="/wiki/Thomism" title="Thomism">Thomism</a> as worthy of continued reform, and as superior to modern trends such as <a href="/wiki/Phenomenology_(philosophy)" title="Phenomenology (philosophy)">phenomenology</a> or <a href="/wiki/Existentialism" title="Existentialism">existentialism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-270" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-270">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Illness_and_death">Illness and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Illness and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
	<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:1983pasca.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/1983pasca.jpg/150px-1983pasca.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="190" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/1983pasca.jpg/225px-1983pasca.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/1983pasca.jpg/300px-1983pasca.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1521" data-file-height="1922" /></a><figcaption>Mother <a href="/wiki/Pascalina_Lehnert" title="Pascalina Lehnert">Pascalina Lehnert</a>, Pius XII's housekeeper and confidant for 41 years, until his death<sup id="cite_ref-Gerard_Noel,_p.9_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gerard_Noel,_p.9-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_439.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_439.jpg/220px-Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_439.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="160" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_439.jpg/330px-Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_439.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_439.jpg/440px-Correio_da_Manh%C3%A3_AN_439.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1238" data-file-height="901" /></a><figcaption>Photograph of Pius XII on his deathbed in Castel Gandolfo, taken on 10 October 1958.</figcaption></figure>
<p>With frequent absences from work, Pope Pius XII had come to depend heavily on a few close colleagues, especially his aide <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Tardini" title="Domenico Tardini">Domenico Tardini</a>, his speechwriter <a href="/wiki/Robert_Leiber" title="Robert Leiber">Robert Leiber</a>, and his long-serving housekeeper <a href="/wiki/Pascalina_Lehnert" title="Pascalina Lehnert">Sister Pascalina Lehnert</a>. The Pope still addressed lay people and groups about a wide range of topics. Sometimes he answered specific moral questions, which were addressed to him. To professional associations he explained specific occupational ethics in light of church teachings. Robert Leiber helped him occasionally with his speeches and publications. <a href="/wiki/Augustin_Bea" title="Augustin Bea">Cardinal Augustine Bea SJ</a> was his personal confessor. Sister Pascalina was for forty years his "housekeeper, muse and lifelong companion".<sup id="cite_ref-271" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-271">&#91;270&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On Monday, 6 October 1958, at around 8:30 AM (07:30 GMT), he suffered a stroke, weakening him greatly in addition to his other maladies, after having taken ill the previous day after a series of meetings. He received the <a href="/wiki/Last_rites" title="Last rites">Last rites</a>. However, his condition suitably improved until 8 October when he suffered a second stroke. By the mid-afternoon, his doctors had reported that Pius XII was suffering from a severe cardio-pulmonary collapse and by 3:00 PM (14:00 GMT) believed that his death was imminent. Just before sunset, Pius XII contracted pneumonia and doctors immediately moved to bring in oxygen and blood plasma. His last words were reportedly, "Pray. Pray that this regrettable situation for the church may end". When Pius XII was interred, the small crucifix and rosary that he held in his hands as he died were buried with him.<sup id="cite_ref-272" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-272">&#91;271&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On the last full day of his life, his temperature rose steadily and his breathing became difficult. At 3:52 AM (02:52 GMT) on Thursday, 9 October, a Feast of Saint Denis of Paris, he gave a smile, lowered his head and died. The cause of death was recorded as acute <a href="/wiki/Heart_failure" title="Heart failure">heart failure</a>. Domenico Tardini prayed the <i>Magnificat Anima mea dominum</i>, the Virgin Mary's praise of the Lord, in Latin. His doctor Gaspanini said afterwards: "The Holy Father did not die because of any specific illness. He was completely exhausted. He was overworked beyond limit. His heart was healthy, his lungs were good. He could have lived another 20 years, had he spared himself."<sup id="cite_ref-273" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-273">&#91;272&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a> declared ten days of mourning;<sup id="cite_ref-delpher.nl_274-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-delpher.nl-274">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a> declared three days of mourning and the closure of offices and schools as a sign of respect;<sup id="cite_ref-275" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-275">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cuba" title="Cuba">Cuba</a> declared three days of mourning.<sup id="cite_ref-delpher.nl_274-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-delpher.nl-274">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Testament of Pope Pius XII was published the month of his death.<sup id="cite_ref-276" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-276">&#91;275&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Botched_embalming">Botched embalming</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: Botched embalming">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tumulo_pio_xii.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Tumulo_pio_xii.jpg/220px-Tumulo_pio_xii.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Tumulo_pio_xii.jpg/330px-Tumulo_pio_xii.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Tumulo_pio_xii.jpg/440px-Tumulo_pio_xii.jpg 2x" data-file-width="480" data-file-height="360" /></a><figcaption><i>The Pope of Mary</i>: A <a href="/wiki/Madonna_and_Child" class="mw-redirect" title="Madonna and Child">Madonna and Child</a>, added by John Paul II in 1982, hangs over the tomb of Pius XII.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Pius XII's physician, <a href="/wiki/Riccardo_Galeazzi-Lisi" title="Riccardo Galeazzi-Lisi">Riccardo Galeazzi-Lisi</a>, reported that the pontiff's body was embalmed in the room where he died using a novel process invented by Oreste Nuzzi.<sup id="cite_ref-ModernMummies_277-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ModernMummies-277">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pope Pius XII did not want the vital organs removed from his body, demanding instead that it be kept in the same condition "in which God created it".<sup id="cite_ref-AP_11Oct58_278-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AP_11Oct58-278">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup> According to Galeazzi-Lisi, this was the reason why he and Nuzzi, an embalmer from <a href="/wiki/Naples" title="Naples">Naples</a>, used an atypical approach with the embalming procedure.<sup id="cite_ref-AP_11Oct58_278-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AP_11Oct58-278">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup> In a controversial press conference, Galeazzi-Lisi described in great detail the embalming of the body of the late pontiff. He claimed to have used the same system of oils and resins with which the body of Jesus Christ was preserved.<sup id="cite_ref-AP_11Oct58_278-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AP_11Oct58-278">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The text near this tag may need clarification or removal of jargon. (July 2017)">clarification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Galeazzi-Lisi asserted that the new process would "preserve the body indefinitely in its natural state".<sup id="cite_ref-ModernMummies_277-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ModernMummies-277">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup> However, whatever chance the new embalming process had of efficaciously preserving the body was obliterated by intense heat in Castel Gandolfo during the embalming process. As a result, the body decomposed rapidly and the viewing of the faithful had to be terminated abruptly.<sup id="cite_ref-279" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-279">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Galeazzi-Lisi reported that heat in the halls, where the body of the late Pope lay in state, caused chemical reactions which required it to be treated twice after the original preparation.<sup id="cite_ref-AP_11Oct58_278-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AP_11Oct58-278">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup> Swiss Guards stationed around Pius XII's body were reported to have become ill during their vigil.<sup id="cite_ref-ModernMummies_277-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ModernMummies-277">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Funeral">Funeral</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: Funeral">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>His funeral procession into Rome was the largest congregation of Romans as of that date. Romans mourned "their" pope, who was born in their own city, especially as a hero in the time of war.<sup id="cite_ref-280" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-280">&#91;279&#93;</a></sup> Cardinal Angelo Giuseppe Roncalli (later to be <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">Pope John XXIII</a>) wrote in his diary on Saturday, 11 October 1958 that probably no Roman emperor had enjoyed such a triumph, which he viewed as a reflection of the spiritual majesty and religious dignity of the late Pius XII.<sup id="cite_ref-281" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-281">&#91;280&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Cause_for_canonisation">Cause for canonisation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=48" title="Edit section: Cause for canonisation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above n" style="background-color:gold;"><div class="honorific-prefix" style="display:inline;font-size: 77%; font-weight: normal;"><a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a></div><br /><div style="display:inline;" class="fn">Pius XII</div><br /><div class="honorific-prefix" style="display:inline;font-size: 77%; font-weight: normal;"><a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Rome" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Rome">Bishop of Rome</a></div></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><span class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Frameless"><a href="/wiki/File:Pio_XII_-_Luis_Fern%C3%A1ndez-Laguna_1958.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Pio_XII_-_Luis_Fern%C3%A1ndez-Laguna_1958.jpg/220px-Pio_XII_-_Luis_Fern%C3%A1ndez-Laguna_1958.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="339" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Pio_XII_-_Luis_Fern%C3%A1ndez-Laguna_1958.jpg/330px-Pio_XII_-_Luis_Fern%C3%A1ndez-Laguna_1958.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Pio_XII_-_Luis_Fern%C3%A1ndez-Laguna_1958.jpg/440px-Pio_XII_-_Luis_Fern%C3%A1ndez-Laguna_1958.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2075" data-file-height="3200" /></a></span></td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background-color:gold;"><a href="/wiki/Pope" title="Pope">Pope</a> and <a href="/wiki/Confessor" title="Confessor">Confessor</a></th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Born</th><td class="infobox-data">2 March 1876<br /><a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Kingdom of Italy</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Died</th><td class="infobox-data">9 October 1958 (aged 82)<br /><a href="/wiki/Castel_Gandolfo" title="Castel Gandolfo">Castel Gandolfo</a>, Rome, <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Venerated&#160;in</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Church">Roman Catholic Church</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Saint_symbolism" title="Saint symbolism">Attributes</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Papal_vestments" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal vestments">Papal vestments</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Papal_tiara" title="Papal tiara">Papal tiara</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Pectoral_cross" title="Pectoral cross">Pectoral cross</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Canonization_of_Pope_Pius_XII" title="Canonization of Pope Pius XII">Canonization of Pope Pius XII</a></div>
<p>Pope Pius XII's <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">cause of canonization</a> was opened on 18 November 1965 by Pope Paul VI during the final session of the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a>. In May 2007, the congregation recommended that Pius XII should be declared <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-282" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-282">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> did so on 19 December 2009, simultaneously making the same declaration in regard to Pope John Paul II.<sup id="cite_ref-Pitel_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pitel-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For Venerable status, the Congregation for the Causes of Saints certifies the "heroic virtues" of the candidate. Making Pius XII as Venerable met with various responses, most centered on the papal words and actions during World War II. Benedict's signature on the decree of heroic virtue was regarded by some as a public relations blunder, though acceptance of Pius XII as a saviour of Europe's Jews is regarded as 'proof of fidelity to the Church, the <i>pope</i> and the Tradition' by neoconservative Catholic groups.<sup id="cite_ref-283" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-283">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup> On the other hand, Rabbi <a href="/wiki/Marvin_Hier" title="Marvin Hier">Marvin Hier</a>, founder and dean at the <a href="/wiki/Simon_Wiesenthal_Center" title="Simon Wiesenthal Center">Simon Wiesenthal Center</a> said "there would be a great distortion of history" if Pius XII were canonized.<sup id="cite_ref-afp_284-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-afp-284">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup> Rabbi Jeremy Lawrence, the head of <a href="/wiki/Great_Synagogue_(Sydney)" title="Great Synagogue (Sydney)">Sydney's Great Synagogue</a>, said: "How can one venerate a man who ... seemed to give his passive permission to the Nazis as the Jews were prised from his doorstep in Rome?"<sup id="cite_ref-maley_285-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maley-285">&#91;284&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 1 August 2013, an anonymous "source who works for the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a>" said <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> is considering canonization without a miracle, "us[ing] the formula of <i>scientia certa</i>".<sup id="cite_ref-286" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-286">&#91;285&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pope Francis also announced his intention in January 2014 to open the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Secret_Archives" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican Secret Archives">Vatican Secret Archives</a> to scholars so that an evaluation of the late pontiff's role in the war can be determined before canonization. This has been met with praise by the Jewish community. However, it was said that it could take up to a year to gather all the documents and then analyze them.<sup id="cite_ref-287" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-287">&#91;286&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-288" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-288">&#91;287&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-289" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-289">&#91;288&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 26 May 2014 on his way back from the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Land" title="Holy Land">Holy Land</a> to <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City</a>, Pope Francis stated that the late pope would not be beatified because the cause has stalled. Pope Francis stated that he checked the progress of the cause for Pius XII and said that there were no miracles attributed to his intercession, which was the main reason that the cause had halted.<sup id="cite_ref-290" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-290">&#91;289&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Father Peter Gumpel stated, on a 12 January 2016 documentary on the late pope, that there was consultation of the Vatican Secret Archives which were carried out in secret; in short it means that there are no controversies surrounding the late pontiff that could impede the potential beatification.<sup id="cite_ref-291" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-291">&#91;290&#93;</a></sup> In that same documentary, the cause's vice-postulator Marc Lindeijer stated that several miracles attributed to the late pope are reported to the postulation every year but the individuals related to the healings do not come forward to enact diocesan proceedings of investigation. Lindeijer explained that this was the reason that the cause has stalled in the past as none have come forward to assist the postulation in their investigations.<sup id="cite_ref-292" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-292">&#91;291&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Potential_miracle">Potential miracle</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=49" title="Edit section: Potential miracle">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Reports from 2014 indicate a potential miracle from the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a> attributed to the intercession of the late pope that was reported to the postulation. The miracle pertains to a male plagued with severe influenza and pneumonia that could have proven to be fatal; the individual was said to have been healed in full after a novena to Pius XII.<sup id="cite_ref-293" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-293">&#91;292&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-294" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-294">&#91;293&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Views.2C_interpretations_and_scholarship"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Views,_interpretations_and_scholarship">Views, interpretations and scholarship</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=50" title="Edit section: Views, interpretations and scholarship">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Contemporary">Contemporary</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=51" title="Edit section: Contemporary">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+link+.ambox{margin-top:-1px}html body.mediawiki .mw-parser-output .ambox.mbox-small-left{margin:4px 1em 4px .ambox-delete{border-left:10px solid #b32424}.mw-parser-output .ambox-content{border-left:10px solid #f28500}.mw-parser-output .ambox-style{border-left:10px solid #fc3}.mw-parser-output .ambox-move{border-left:10px solid #9932cc}.mw-parser-output .ambox-protection{border-left:10px solid #a2a9b1}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-text{border:none;padding:0.25em 0.5em;width:100%}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-image{border:none;padding:2px 0 2px 0.5em;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-imageright{border:none;padding:2px 0.5em 2px
