.mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-POV plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-POV" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><span><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Unbalanced_scales.svg/45px-Unbalanced_scales.svg.png" decoding="async" width="45" height="40" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Unbalanced_scales.svg/68px-Unbalanced_scales.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Unbalanced_scales.svg/90px-Unbalanced_scales.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="400" data-file-height="354" /></span></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">The <b><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Neutral_point_of_view" title="Wikipedia:Neutral point of view">neutrality</a> of this section is <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NPOV_dispute" title="Wikipedia:NPOV dispute">disputed</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Relevant discussion may be found on the <a href="/wiki/Talk:Pope_Pius_XII##" title="Talk:Pope Pius XII">talk page</a>. Please do not remove this message until <a href="/wiki/Template:POV#When_to_remove" title="Template:POV">conditions to do so are met</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">April 2015</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>During the war, <i><a href="/wiki/Time_(magazine)" title="Time (magazine)">Time</a></i> credited Pius XII and the Catholic Church for "fighting <a href="/wiki/Totalitarianism" title="Totalitarianism">totalitarianism</a> more knowingly, devoutly and authoritatively, and for a longer time, than any other organised power".<sup id="cite_ref-295" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-295">&#91;294&#93;</a></sup> During the war he was also praised editorially by <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_York_Times" title="The New York Times">The New York Times</a></i> for opposing Nazi anti-Semitism and aggression.<sup id="cite_ref-296" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-296">&#91;295&#93;</a></sup> According to Paul O'Shea, "The Nazis demonised the Pope as the agent of international Jewry; the Americans and British were continually frustrated because he would not condemn Nazi aggression; and the Russians accused him of being an agent of Fascism and the Nazis."<sup id="cite_ref-297" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-297">&#91;296&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 21 September 1945, the general secretary of the World Jewish Council, Leon Kubowitzky, presented an amount of money to the pope, "in recognition of the work of the Holy See in rescuing Jews from Fascist and Nazi persecutions."<sup id="cite_ref-298" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-298">&#91;297&#93;</a></sup> After the war, in the autumn of 1945, Harry Greenstein from <a href="/wiki/Baltimore" title="Baltimore">Baltimore</a>, a close friend of Chief Rabbi Herzog of <a href="/wiki/Jerusalem" title="Jerusalem">Jerusalem</a>, told Pius XII how grateful Jews were for all he had done for them. "My only regret", the pope replied, "is not to have been able to save a greater number of Jews".<sup id="cite_ref-299" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-299">&#91;298&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius XII was also criticised during his lifetime. <a href="/wiki/Leon_Poliakov" class="mw-redirect" title="Leon Poliakov">Leon Poliakov</a> wrote in 1950 that Pius XII had been a tacit supporter of <a href="/wiki/Vichy_France" title="Vichy France">Vichy France</a>'s anti-Semitic laws, calling him "less forthright" than <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a> either out of "Germanophilia" or the hope that Hitler would defeat Communist Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-300" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-300">&#91;299&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After Pius XII's death on 9 October 1958 many Jewish organisations and newspapers around the world paid tribute to his legacy. At the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, <a href="/wiki/Golda_Meir" title="Golda Meir">Golda Meir</a>, Israel's Foreign Minister, said, "When fearful martyrdom came to our people in the decade of Nazi terror, the voice of the Pope was raised for the victims. The life of our times was enriched by a voice speaking out on the great moral truths above the tumult of daily conflict."<sup id="cite_ref-ewtn_301-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn-301">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup> <i>The Jewish Chronicle</i> (London) stated on 10 October, "Adherents of all creeds and parties will recall how Pius XII faced the responsibilities of his exalted office with courage and devotion. Before, during, and after the Second World War, he constantly preached the message of peace. Confronted by the monstrous cruelties of <a href="/wiki/Nazism" title="Nazism">Nazism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fascism" title="Fascism">Fascism</a> and Communism, he repeatedly proclaimed the virtues of humanity and compassion."<sup id="cite_ref-ewtn_301-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn-301">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup> In the Canadian Jewish Chronicle (17 October), Rabbi J. Stern stated that Pius XII "made it possible for thousands of Jewish victims of Nazism and Fascism to be hidden away..."<sup id="cite_ref-ewtn_301-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn-301">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup> In 6 November edition of the Jewish Post in <a href="/wiki/Winnipeg" title="Winnipeg">Winnipeg</a>, William Zukerman, the former American Hebrew columnist, wrote that no other leader "did more to help the Jews in their hour of greatest tragedy, during the Nazi occupation of Europe, than the late Pope".<sup id="cite_ref-ewtn_301-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn-301">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup> Other prominent Jewish figures, such as Israeli Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Moshe_Sharett" title="Moshe Sharett">Moshe Sharett</a> and Chief Rabbi <a href="/wiki/Isaac_Herzog" title="Isaac Herzog">Isaac Herzog</a> expressed their public gratitude to Pius XII.<sup id="cite_ref-302" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-302">&#91;301&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_historical_accounts">Early historical accounts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=52" title="Edit section: Early historical accounts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Some early works echoed the favourable sentiments of the war period, including Polish historian <a href="/wiki/Oskar_Halecki" title="Oskar Halecki">Oskar Halecki</a>'s <i>Pius XII: Eugenio Pacelli: Pope of peace</i> (1954) and Nazareno Padellaro's <i>Portrait of Pius XII</i> (1949).
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Pinchas_Lapide" title="Pinchas Lapide">Pinchas Lapide</a>, a Jewish theologian and <a href="/wiki/Israel" title="Israel">Israeli</a> diplomat to <a href="/wiki/Milan" title="Milan">Milan</a> in the 1960s, estimated controversially in <i><a href="/wiki/Three_Popes_and_the_Jews" title="Three Popes and the Jews">Three Popes and the Jews</a></i> that Pius "was instrumental in saving at least 700,000 but probably as many as 860,000 Jews from certain death at Nazi hands".<sup id="cite_ref-303" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-303">&#91;302&#93;</a></sup> Some historians have questioned this<sup id="cite_ref-304" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-304">&#91;303&#93;</a></sup> often cited number, which Lapide reached by "deducting all reasonable claims of rescue" by non-Catholics from the total number of European Jews surviving the <a href="/wiki/The_Holocaust" title="The Holocaust">Holocaust</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-305" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-305">&#91;304&#93;</a></sup> A Catholic scholar, Kevin Madigan, has interpreted this and other praise from prominent Jewish leaders, including that offered by Meir, as less than sincere, an attempt to secure Vatican <a href="/wiki/International_recognition_of_Israel" title="International recognition of Israel">recognition of the State of Israel</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-306" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-306">&#91;305&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_Deputy"><i>The Deputy</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=53" title="Edit section: The Deputy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/The_Deputy" title="The Deputy">The Deputy</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:1899eugenio.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/1899eugenio.jpg/220px-1899eugenio.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="269" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/1899eugenio.jpg/330px-1899eugenio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/1899eugenio.jpg/440px-1899eugenio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="694" data-file-height="849" /></a><figcaption>A rare 1899 handwriting of Eugenio Pacelli with text in <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1963, <a href="/wiki/Rolf_Hochhuth" title="Rolf Hochhuth">Rolf Hochhuth</a>'s controversial drama <i><a href="/wiki/The_Deputy" title="The Deputy">Der Stellvertreter. Ein christliches Trauerspiel</a></i> (<i>The Deputy, a Christian tragedy</i>, released in English in 1964) portrayed Pope Pius XII as a hypocrite who remained silent about the Holocaust. The depiction is described as lacking "credible substantiation" by the <a href="/wiki/Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica" title="Encyclopædia Britannica">Encyclopædia Britannica</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-307" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-307">&#91;306&#93;</a></sup> Books such as Joseph Lichten's <i>A Question of Judgment</i> (1963), written in response to <i>The Deputy</i>, defended Pius XII's actions during the war. Lichten labelled any criticism of the pope's actions during World War II as "a stupefying paradox" and said, "no one who reads the record of Pius XII's actions on behalf of Jews can subscribe to Hochhuth's accusation".<sup id="cite_ref-308" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-308">&#91;307&#93;</a></sup> Critical scholarly works like <a href="/wiki/Guenter_Lewy" title="Guenter Lewy">Guenter Lewy</a>'s controversial <i>The Catholic Church and Nazi Germany</i> (1964) also followed the publication of <i>The Deputy</i>. Lewy's conclusion was that "the Pope and his advisers — influenced by the long tradition of moderate anti-Semitism so widely accepted in Vatican circles — did not view the plight of the Jews with a real sense of urgency and moral outrage. For this assertion no documentation is possible, but it is a conclusion difficult to avoid".<sup id="cite_ref-309" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-309">&#91;308&#93;</a></sup> In 2002 the play was adapted into the film, <i><a href="/wiki/Amen." title="Amen.">Amen.</a></i>. An article on <i><a href="/wiki/La_Civilit%C3%A0_Cattolica" class="mw-redirect" title="La Civilità Cattolica">La Civilità Cattolica</a></i> in March 2009 indicated the accusations that Hochhuth's play made widely known originated not among Jews but in the <a href="/wiki/Communist_bloc" class="mw-redirect" title="Communist bloc">Communist bloc</a>. It was on Moscow Radio, on 2 June 1945, that the first accusation directly against Pius XII of refusing to speak out against the exterminations in Nazi concentration camps. It was also the first medium to call him "Hitler's Pope".<sup id="cite_ref-310" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-310">&#91;309&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The former high-ranking <a href="/wiki/Securitate" title="Securitate">Securitate</a> General <a href="/wiki/Ion_Mihai_Pacepa" title="Ion Mihai Pacepa">Ion Mihai Pacepa</a> alleged in 2007 that Hochhuth's play and numerous publications attacking Pius XII as a Nazi sympathizer were fabrications that were part of a <a href="/wiki/KGB" title="KGB">KGB</a> and Eastern bloc secret services <a href="/wiki/Disinformation" title="Disinformation">disinformation</a> campaign, named <a href="/wiki/Seat_12" title="Seat 12">Seat 12</a>, to discredit the <a href="/wiki/Moral_authority" title="Moral authority">moral authority</a> of the church and Christianity in the west.<sup id="cite_ref-article.nationalreview.com_311-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-article.nationalreview.com-311">&#91;310&#93;</a></sup> Pacepa indicated that he was involved in contacting eastern bloc agents close the Vatican in order to fabricate the story to be used for the attack against the wartime pope.<sup id="cite_ref-article.nationalreview.com_311-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-article.nationalreview.com-311">&#91;310&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Actes"><i>Actes</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=54" title="Edit section: Actes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Actes_et_documents_du_Saint_Si%C3%A8ge_relatifs_%C3%A0_la_Seconde_Guerre_Mondiale" title="Actes et documents du Saint Siège relatifs à la Seconde Guerre Mondiale">Actes et documents du Saint Siège relatifs à la Seconde Guerre Mondiale</a></div>
<p>In the aftermath of the controversy surrounding <i>The Deputy</i>, in 1964, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a> authorized Jesuit scholars to access the Vatican State Department Archives, which are normally not opened for seventy-five years. Original documents in French and Italian, <i><a href="/wiki/Actes_et_documents_du_Saint_Si%C3%A8ge_relatifs_%C3%A0_la_Seconde_Guerre_Mondiale" title="Actes et documents du Saint Siège relatifs à la Seconde Guerre Mondiale">Actes et documents du Saint Siège relatifs à la Seconde Guerre Mondiale</a></i>, were published in eleven volumes between 1965 and 1981.<sup id="cite_ref-30giorni.it_216-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30giorni.it-216">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> The volumes were edited by Four Jesuits: Angelo Martini, Burkhart Schneider, Robert Graham and Pierre Blet.<sup id="cite_ref-L&#39;oro_di_Pio_XII_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-L&#39;oro_di_Pio_XII-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Blet also published a summary of the eleven volumes.<sup id="cite_ref-312" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-312">&#91;311&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Hitler.27s_Pope_and_The_Myth_of_Hitler.27s_Pope"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Hitler's_Pope_and_The_Myth_of_Hitler's_Pope"><i>Hitler's Pope</i> and <i>The Myth of Hitler's Pope</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=55" title="Edit section: Hitler&#039;s Pope and The Myth of Hitler&#039;s Pope">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Hitler%27s_Pope" title="Hitler&#39;s Pope">Hitler's Pope</a> and <a href="/wiki/The_Myth_of_Hitler%27s_Pope" title="The Myth of Hitler&#39;s Pope">The Myth of Hitler's Pope</a></div>
<p>In 1999, British author <a href="/wiki/John_Cornwell_(writer)" title="John Cornwell (writer)">John Cornwell</a>'s <i>Hitler's Pope</i> criticised Pius XII for his actions and inactions during the Holocaust. Cornwell argued that Pius XII subordinated opposition to the Nazis to his goal of increasing and centralising the power of the Papacy. Further, Cornwell accused Pius XII of anti-Semitism.<sup id="cite_ref-313" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-313">&#91;312&#93;</a></sup> The <i><a href="/wiki/Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica" title="Encyclopædia Britannica">Encyclopædia Britannica</a></i> described Cornwell's depiction of Pius XII as anti-Semitic as lacking "credible substantiation".<sup id="cite_ref-314" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-314">&#91;313&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Kenneth_L._Woodward" title="Kenneth L. Woodward">Kenneth L. Woodward</a> stated in his review in <i>Newsweek</i> that "errors of fact and ignorance of context appear on almost every page".<sup id="cite_ref-315" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-315">&#91;314&#93;</a></sup> Paul O'Shea summarized the work by saying it was "disappointing because of its many inaccuracies, selective use of sources, and claims that do not bear any scrutiny. However, [Cornwell] has rendered a service by insisting Pacelli be re-examined thoroughly and placed firmly within the context of his times".<sup id="cite_ref-316" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-316">&#91;315&#93;</a></sup> Five years after the publication of <i>Hitler's Pope</i>, Cornwell stated: "I would now argue, in the light of the debates and evidence following <i>Hitler's Pope</i>, that Pius XII had so little scope of action that it is impossible to judge the motives for his silence during the war, while Rome was under the heel of Mussolini and later occupied by Germany".<sup id="cite_ref-317" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-317">&#91;316&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-318" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-318">&#91;317&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-319" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-319">&#91;318&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cornwell's work was the first to have access to testimonies from Pius XII's <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a> process as well as to many documents from Pacelli's nunciature which had just been opened under the 75-year rule by the Vatican State Secretary archives.<sup id="cite_ref-320" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-320">&#91;319&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Susan_Zuccotti" title="Susan Zuccotti">Susan Zuccotti</a>'s <i><a href="/wiki/Under_His_Very_Windows:_The_Vatican_and_the_Holocaust_in_Italy" class="mw-redirect" title="Under His Very Windows: The Vatican and the Holocaust in Italy">Under His Very Windows: The Vatican and the Holocaust in Italy</a></i> (2000) and <a href="/wiki/Michael_Phayer" title="Michael Phayer">Michael Phayer</a>'s <i>The Catholic Church and the Holocaust, 1930–1965</i> (2000) and <i><a href="/wiki/Pius_XII,_The_Holocaust,_and_the_Cold_War" title="Pius XII, The Holocaust, and the Cold War">Pius XII, The Holocaust, and the Cold War</a></i> (2008) provided further critical, though more scholarly analysis of Pius's legacy.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica2_321-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica2-321">&#91;320&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Goldhagen" title="Daniel Goldhagen">Daniel Goldhagen</a>'s <i>A Moral Reckoning</i> and <a href="/wiki/David_Kertzer" title="David Kertzer">David Kertzer</a>'s <i>The Pope Against the Jews</i> denounced Pius, while Ralph McInery and José Sanchez wrote less critical assessments of Pius XII's pontificate.<sup id="cite_ref-The_Pope_was_wrong_322-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Pope_was_wrong-322">&#91;321&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In specific riposte to Cornwell's criticism, American Rabbi and historian <a href="/wiki/David_Dalin" class="mw-redirect" title="David Dalin">David Dalin</a> published <i><a href="/wiki/The_Myth_of_Hitler%27s_Pope" title="The Myth of Hitler&#39;s Pope">The Myth of Hitler's Pope: How Pope Pius XII Rescued Jews from the Nazis</a></i> in 2005. He reaffirmed previous accounts of Pius having been a saviour of thousands of Europe's Jews. In a review of the book, another Jewish scholar — Churchill biographer, <a href="/wiki/Martin_Gilbert" title="Martin Gilbert">Martin Gilbert</a> — wrote that Dalin's work was "an essential contribution to our understanding of the reality of Pope Pius XII's support for Jews at their time of greatest danger. Hopefully, his account will replace the divisively harmful version of papal neglect, and even collaboration, that has held the field for far too long".<sup id="cite_ref-323" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-323">&#91;322&#93;</a></sup> Dalin's book also argued that Cornwell and others were liberal Catholics and ex-Catholics who "exploit the tragedy of the Jewish people during the Holocaust to foster their own political agenda of forcing changes on the Catholic Church today" and that Pius XII was responsible for saving the lives of many thousands of Jews.<sup id="cite_ref-324" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-324">&#91;323&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A number of other scholars replied with favourable accounts of Pius XII, including <a href="/wiki/Margherita_Marchione" title="Margherita Marchione">Margherita Marchione</a>'s <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Yours_Is_a_Precious_Witness&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Yours Is a Precious Witness (page does not exist)">Yours Is a Precious Witness</a>: Memoirs of Jews and Catholics in Wartime Italy</i> (1997), <i>Pope Pius XII: Architect for Peace</i> (2000) and <i>Consensus and Controversy: Defending Pope Pius XII</i> (2002); <a href="/w/index.php?title=Pierre_Blet&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Pierre Blet (page does not exist)">Pierre Blet</a>'s <i>Pius XII and the Second World War, According to the Archives of the Vatican</i> (1999); and <a href="/wiki/Ronald_J._Rychlak" title="Ronald J. Rychlak">Ronald J. Rychlak</a>'s <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Hitler,_the_War_and_the_Pope&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Hitler, the War and the Pope (page does not exist)">Hitler, the War and the Pope</a></i> (2000).<sup id="cite_ref-britannica2_321-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica2-321">&#91;320&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-325" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-325">&#91;324&#93;</a></sup> Ecclesiastical historian William Doino (author of <i>The Pius War: Responses to the Critics of Pius XII</i>), concluded that Pius was "emphatically <i>not</i> silent".<sup id="cite_ref-326" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-326">&#91;325&#93;</a></sup> Other important works challenging the negative characterization of Pius's legacy were written by <a href="/wiki/Eamon_Duffy" title="Eamon Duffy">Eamon Duffy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Clifford_Longley" title="Clifford Longley">Clifford Longley</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Winning" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal Winning">Cardinal Winning</a>, <a href="/wiki/Michael_Burleigh" title="Michael Burleigh">Michael Burleigh</a>, Paul Johnson, and <a href="/wiki/Denis_Mack_Smith" title="Denis Mack Smith">Denis Mack Smith</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-The_Pope_was_wrong_322-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Pope_was_wrong-322">&#91;321&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In his 2003 book <i><a href="/wiki/A_Moral_Reckoning" title="A Moral Reckoning">A Moral Reckoning</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Goldhagen" title="Daniel Goldhagen">Daniel Goldhagen</a> asserted that Pius XII "chose again and again not to mention the Jews publicly.... [In] public statements by Pius XII ... any mention of the Jews is conspicuously absent." In a review of Goldhagen's book, <a href="/wiki/Mark_Riebling" title="Mark Riebling">Mark Riebling</a> counters that Pius used the word "Jew" in his first encyclical, <i><a href="/wiki/Summi_Pontificatus" title="Summi Pontificatus">Summi Pontificatus</a></i>, published on 20 October 1939. "There Pius insisted that all human beings be treated charitably — for, as <a href="/wiki/Saint_Paul" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Paul">Paul</a> had written to the <a href="/wiki/Colossians" class="mw-redirect" title="Colossians">Colossians</a>, in God's eyes "there is neither Gentile nor Jew". In saying this, the Pope affirmed that Jews were full members of the human community — which is Goldhagen's own criterion for establishing 'dissent from the anti-Semitic creed'."<sup id="cite_ref-327" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-327">&#91;326&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In <i>Pius XII, the Hound of Hitler</i>, Catholic journalist <a href="/w/index.php?title=Gerard_Noel_(author)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Gerard Noel (author) (page does not exist)">Gerard Noel</a> dismissed accusations that Pius was "anti-semitic" or "pro-Nazi", but accused him of "silence" based on fear of retaliation and wrote that "Hitler played the Pope with consummate expertise".<sup id="cite_ref-The_Pope_was_wrong_322-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Pope_was_wrong-322">&#91;321&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Ian_Kershaw" title="Ian Kershaw">Ian Kershaw</a> came to a similar conclusion about Pius's motives.<sup id="cite_ref-kershaw_328-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kershaw-328">&#91;327&#93;</a></sup> He suggested that besides seeking to protect his own church and parishioners, Pius feared that speaking out would worsen the plight of the Jews, though he could have hardly made it worse after 1942. Kershaw called the 1942 Christmas message "a missed opportunity", adding: "Having decided to refer to the genocide, Pius ought to have followed this with a condemnation that was loud, plain and unequivocal." However, he doubted that condemnation from the Pope would have led to Nazi Germany changing course.<sup id="cite_ref-kershaw_328-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kershaw-328">&#91;327&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Gerald_Steinacher" title="Gerald Steinacher">Gerald Steinacher</a>'s <i>Nazis on the Run</i> accused Pius of turning a blind eye to the activities of Vatican priests assisting "denazification through conversion", which he said helped ex-Nazi anti-communists to escape justice.<sup id="cite_ref-329" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-329">&#91;328&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-330" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-330">&#91;329&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A Berlin Jewish couple, Mr. and Mrs. Wolfsson, argued in defense of the pope: "None of us wanted the Pope to take an open stand. We were all fugitives, and fugitives do not wish to be pointed at. The Gestapo would have become more excited and would have intensified its inquisitions. If the Pope had protested, Rome would have become the center of attention. It was better that the Pope said nothing. We all shared this opinion at the time, and this is still our conviction today." There were examples when the Catholic Church reaction to Nazi brutality only intensified SS persecutions of both Jews and the church.<sup id="cite_ref-331" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-331">&#91;330&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="International_Catholic-Jewish_Historical_Commission">International Catholic-Jewish Historical Commission</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=56" title="Edit section: International Catholic-Jewish Historical Commission">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/International_Catholic-Jewish_Historical_Commission" class="mw-redirect" title="International Catholic-Jewish Historical Commission">International Catholic-Jewish Historical Commission</a></div>
<p>In 1999, in an attempt to address some of this controversy, the International Catholic-Jewish Historical Commission (Historical Commission), a group of three Catholic and three Jewish scholars was appointed, respectively, by the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a>'s Commission for Religious Relations with the Jews (Holy See's Commission) and the International Jewish Committee for Interreligious Consultations (IJCIC), to whom a preliminary report was issued in October 2000.<sup id="cite_ref-ICJHC_332-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ICJHC-332">&#91;331&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Commission did not discover any documents, but had the agreed-upon task to review the existing Vatican volumes, that make up the <i>Actes et Documents du Saint Siège (ADSS)</i><sup id="cite_ref-333" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-333">&#91;332&#93;</a></sup> The commission was internally divided over the question of access to additional documents from the Holy See, access to the news media by individual commission members, and, questions to be raised in the preliminary report. It was agreed to include all 47 individual questions by the six members, and use them as Preliminary Report.<sup id="cite_ref-Gerard_P_Fogarty,_Vatican/Holocaust_334-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gerard_P_Fogarty,_Vatican/Holocaust-334">&#91;333&#93;</a></sup> In addition to the 47 questions, the commission issued no findings of its own. It stated that it was not their task to sit in judgment of the Pope and his advisors but to contribute to a more nuanced understanding of the papacy during the Holocaust.<sup id="cite_ref-335" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-335">&#91;334&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 47 questions by the six scholars were grouped into three parts: (a) 27 specific questions on existing documents,<sup id="cite_ref-336" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-336">&#91;335&#93;</a></sup> mostly asking for background and additional information such as drafts of the encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Mit_brennender_Sorge" title="Mit brennender Sorge">Mit brennender Sorge</a></i>, which was largely written by Eugenio Pacelli.<sup id="cite_ref-337" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-337">&#91;336&#93;</a></sup> (b) Fourteen questions dealt with themes of individual volumes,<sup id="cite_ref-338" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-338">&#91;337&#93;</a></sup> such as the question how Pius viewed the role of the church during the war.<sup id="cite_ref-339" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-339">&#91;338&#93;</a></sup> (c) Six general questions,<sup id="cite_ref-340" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-340">&#91;339&#93;</a></sup> such as the absence of any anti-communist sentiments in the documents.<sup id="cite_ref-341" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-341">&#91;340&#93;</a></sup> The disagreement between members over additional documents locked up under the Holy See's 70-year rule resulted in a discontinuation of the commission in 2001 on friendly terms.<sup id="cite_ref-Gerard_P_Fogarty,_Vatican/Holocaust_334-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gerard_P_Fogarty,_Vatican/Holocaust-334">&#91;333&#93;</a></sup> Unsatisfied with the findings, Michael Marrus, one of the three Jewish members of the commission, said the commission "ran up against a brick wall .... It would have been really helpful to have had support from the Holy See on this issue."<sup id="cite_ref-342" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-342">&#91;341&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Peter_Stanford" title="Peter Stanford">Peter Stanford</a>, a Catholic journalist and writer, wrote, regarding <i>Fatal Silence: the pope, the resistance and the German occupation of Rome</i> (written by Robert Katz; Weidenfeld &amp; Nicolson, 2003):
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>[The Vatican] still refuses to open all its files from the period — which seems to me to be a conclusive admission of guilt — but Katz has winkled various papers out of God's business address on earth to add to the stash of new information he has uncovered in America in the archives of the Office of Strategic Services. From this we learn that, although Pius's defenders still say that he paid a golden ransom in a vain effort to save Rome's Jews from transportation to the death camps, the most he did was indicate a willingness to chip in if the Jews could not raise the sum demanded. He also shows that no individual Jews were spared, as is often claimed, after Pius personally intervened with the Nazis. Moreover, Katz reveals that those who did escape the Nazi round-up and found sanctuary in church buildings in Rome did so in the face of explicit opposition from the Vatican. The real heroes and heroines were the priests and nuns who refused to bow to Pius's officials and hand over the desperate people whom they were hiding. The main problem with writing about Pius's wartime is that in effect, he did nothing. Facing the murders of six million people, he remained silent. As Jews were taken away from the ghetto that sat right alongside St Peter's, he may have agonised, but he did not intervene. When he did raise his voice with the German occupiers, it was either to ensure that the Vatican City state would not be compromised — that is to say, he would be safe — or to emphasise his own neutrality in a conflict which, for many, became a battle between good and evil. His unrealistic hope was that the Catholic Church could emerge as the peacemaker across Europe. Instead, both the American and British leaderships, as Katz shows, regarded the papacy as tainted by its association with Nazism and irrelevant in the post-1945 reshaping of the continent. Both had urged Pius to speak up against the Holocaust and so drew their own conclusions about him. Far from being a saint, then, he was at best a fool, perhaps an anti-Semite and probably a coward.<sup id="cite_ref-343" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-343">&#91;342&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Katz's book also discusses how the Pope's view of the anti-Nazi resistance — as harbingers of Communism — meant he chose not to intervene in the <a href="/wiki/Ardeatine_massacre" title="Ardeatine massacre">Ardeatine Caves Massacre</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-344" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-344">&#91;343&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Recent_developments">Recent developments</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=57" title="Edit section: Recent developments">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In <i>The Real Odessa. How Peron Brought the Nazi War Criminals to Argentina</i> (2002), the Argentine journalist <a href="/wiki/Uki_Go%C3%B1i" title="Uki Goñi">Uki Goñi</a> described how the Argentinian government dealt with war criminals who entered Argentina. However, during his research Goñi accidentally stumbled on British Foreign Office documents relating to the involvement of Vatican personnel in the smuggling of war criminals, the so-called <a href="/wiki/Ratlines_(history)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ratlines (history)">post-war "ratlines"</a>. Goñi found out that the British Envoy <a href="/wiki/D%27Arcy_Osborne,_12th_Duke_of_Leeds" title="D&#39;Arcy Osborne, 12th Duke of Leeds">D'Arcy Osborne</a> had intervened with Pope Pius XII to put an end to these illegal activities. Furthermore, he discovered "that the Pope secretly pleaded with Washington and London on behalf of notorious criminals and Nazi collaborators".<sup id="cite_ref-345" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-345">&#91;344&#93;</a></sup> Suzanne Brown-Fleming's <i>The Holocaust and Catholic Conscience. Cardinal Aloisius Muench and the Guilt Question in Germany</i> (2006) underlines Goñi's findings. Brown-Fleming stated how Pius XII allegedly intervened on behalf of German war criminals (e.g. <a href="/wiki/Otto_Ohlendorf" title="Otto Ohlendorf">Otto Ohlendorf</a>). Brown-Fleming's main source was the archive of Pope Pius XII's representative in post-war Germany, <a href="/wiki/Aloisius_Joseph_Muench" title="Aloisius Joseph Muench">Cardinal Aloisius Muench</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-346" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-346">&#91;345&#93;</a></sup> Then, Phayer's <i><a href="/wiki/Pius_XII,_The_Holocaust,_and_the_Cold_War" title="Pius XII, The Holocaust, and the Cold War">Pius XII, The Holocaust, and the Cold War</a></i> (2008) utilized documents that were released via <a href="/wiki/Bill_Clinton" title="Bill Clinton">Bill Clinton</a>'s 1997 executive order declassifying wartime and postwar documents, many of which are currently at the <a href="/wiki/US_National_Archives" class="mw-redirect" title="US National Archives">US National Archives</a> and <a href="/wiki/Holocaust_Memorial_Museum" class="mw-redirect" title="Holocaust Memorial Museum">Holocaust Memorial Museum</a>. These documents include diplomatic correspondence, American espionage, and decryptions of German communications. Relevant documents have also been released by the <a href="/wiki/Argentine_government" class="mw-redirect" title="Argentine government">Argentine government</a> and the British Foreign Office. Other information sources have become available, including the diary of Bishop <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Patrick_Hurley" title="Joseph Patrick Hurley">Hurley</a>. These documents reveal new information about Pius XII's actions regarding the <a href="/wiki/Usta%C5%A1e" title="Ustaše">Ustaše</a> regime, the genocides in Poland, the finances of the wartime church, the deportation of the Roman Jews, and the ratlines for Nazis and fascists fleeing Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-347" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-347">&#91;346&#93;</a></sup> According to Phayer, "the face of Pope Pius that we see in these documents is not the same face we see in the eleven volumes the Vatican published of World War II documents, a collection which, though valuable, is nonetheless critically flawed because of its many omissions".<sup id="cite_ref-348" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-348">&#91;347&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 19 September 2008, Pope <a href="/wiki/Benedict_XVI" class="mw-redirect" title="Benedict XVI">Benedict XVI</a> held a reception for conference participants during which he praised Pius XII as a pope who made every effort to save Jews during the war.<sup id="cite_ref-349" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-349">&#91;348&#93;</a></sup> A second conference was held from 6–8 November 2008 by the Pontifical Academy of Life.<sup id="cite_ref-350" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-350">&#91;349&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 9 October 2008, the 50th anniversary of Pius XII's death, Benedict XVI celebrated pontifical Mass in his memory. Shortly before and after the Mass, dialectics continued between the Jewish hierarchy and the Vatican as Rabbi Shear Yeshuv Cohen of <a href="/wiki/Haifa" title="Haifa">Haifa</a> addressed the Synod of Bishops and expressed his disappointment towards Pius XII's "silence" during the war.<sup id="cite_ref-351" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-351">&#91;350&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 16 June 2009, the <i><a href="/wiki/Pave_the_Way_Foundation" title="Pave the Way Foundation">Pave the Way Foundation</a></i> announced that it would release 2,300 pages of documents in <a href="/wiki/Avellino" title="Avellino">Avellino</a>, <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>, dating from 1940 to 1945, which the organisation claims show that Pius XII "worked diligently to save Jews from Nazi tyranny"; the organisation's founder, Krupp, has accused historians of harbouring "private agendas" and having "let down" the public.<sup id="cite_ref-352" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-352">&#91;351&#93;</a></sup> The foundation's research led to the publication of the book <i>Pope Pius XII and World War II: the documented truth</i>, authored by Krupp; the book reproduces 225 pages of the new documents produced by the foundation's research.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Mark_Riebling" title="Mark Riebling">Mark Riebling</a> argued in his 2015 book <i>Church of Spies</i> that Pius XII was involved in plots to overthrow Hitler from mid-October 1939 and was prepared to mediate a peace between the Allies and the Axis in the event of a regime change in Germany. The courier between the resistance group under <a href="/wiki/Wilhelm_Canaris" title="Wilhelm Canaris">Admiral Canaris</a> and the Pope was the Bavarian lawyer and Catholic politician <a href="/wiki/Josef_M%C3%BCller_(CSU_politician)" title="Josef Müller (CSU politician)">Joseph Müller</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-353" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-353">&#91;352&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Opening_of_the_Vatican_Secret_Archives">Opening of the Vatican Secret Archives</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=58" title="Edit section: Opening of the Vatican Secret Archives">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On the occasion of the 80th anniversary of the <a href="/wiki/Appointment_of_Catholic_bishops" title="Appointment of Catholic bishops">appointment</a> of Pius XII as <a href="/wiki/Diocese_of_Rome" title="Diocese of Rome">Bishop of Rome</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> announced during an audience for staff of the Vatican Secret Archives on 4 March 2019 that Vatican archival materials pertaining to Pius XII's pontificate will be accessible to scholars beginning on 2 March 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-354" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-354">&#91;353&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-355" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-355">&#91;354&#93;</a></sup> While this announcement was welcome by researchers, much of it has been clouded by the role of Pope Pius XII with regard to the Holocaust. However, archival research of this period should inform a much broader shift within global Christianity, from Europe to the global South.<sup id="cite_ref-356" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-356">&#91;355&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Vatican archives have provided many millions of pages and it is expected to take many years to process the findings. As of May 2021, the study of the archive has been inconclusive.<sup id="cite_ref-357" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-357">&#91;356&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In January 2022, historian <a href="/wiki/Michael_F._Feldkamp" title="Michael F. Feldkamp">Michael Feldkamp</a> announced that he had discovered in the Vatican archives evidence that Pius XII had personally saved at least 15,000 Jews from extermination, and that he had sent a report on the Holocaust to the American government shortly after the <a href="/wiki/Wannsee_Conference" title="Wannsee Conference">Wannsee Conference</a>, although they did not believe the pope.<sup id="cite_ref-358" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-358">&#91;357&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In June 2022, <a href="/wiki/David_Kertzer" title="David Kertzer">David Kertzer</a>, one of the first historians to have analyzed the archives, published his book <i>The Pope at War</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-359" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-359">&#91;358&#93;</a></sup> Kertzer, with the support of thousands of unpublished documents, uncovered the existence of secret negotiations between Hitler and Pius XII already a few weeks after the end of the <a href="/wiki/Conclave" class="mw-redirect" title="Conclave">conclave</a>, promoted by Hitler himself with the intention of improving his relations with the Vatican. Pius XII, for his part, concentrated his efforts on protecting and improving the situation of the Church in Germany in the face of the <a href="/wiki/Anti-Catholicism" title="Anti-Catholicism">anti-Catholic</a> policies of the Nazis, although no agreement was reached.<sup id="cite_ref-360" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-360">&#91;359&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<li id="cite_note-99"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-99">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">John Paul II</a> surpassed this number on 21 February 2001, elevating 44 cardinals. By that time, the limit had been suspended and over 120 Cardinals existed.</span>
</li>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_XII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=61" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<li id="cite_note-61"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-61">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">On 28 January 1939, eleven days before the death of Pius XI, a disappointed Gundlach informed LaFarge, the encyclical's author, "It cannot go on like this". The text had not been forwarded to the Vatican. He had talked to the American assistant to Father General, who promised to look into the matter in December 1938, but did not report back. Passelecq, Suchecky. p. 121</span>

<li id="cite_note-62"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-62">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Humani generis unitas</span>

<li id="cite_note-63"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-63">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20121030133305/http://www.adl.org/main_Interfaith/nostra_aetate.htm?Multi_page_sections=sHeading_4">"Nostra aetate: Transforming the Catholic-Jewish Relationship: Jewish-Catholic Relationship Transformed"</a>. Adl.org. Archived from <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.adl.org/main_Interfaith/nostra_aetate.htm?Multi_page_sections=sHeading_4">the original</a> on 30 October 2012<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">6 May</span> 2009</span>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=Nostra+aetate%3A+Transforming+the+Catholic-Jewish+Relationship%3A+Jewish-Catholic+Relationship+Transformed&amp;rft.pub=Adl.org&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.adl.org%2Fmain_Interfaith%2Fnostra_aetate.htm%3FMulti_page_sections%3DsHeading_4&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Pius+XII" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<li id="cite_note-64"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-64">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">On 16 March four days after coronation, Gundlach informed LaFarge that the documents had been given to Pius XI shortly before his death, but that the new Pope had so far had no opportunity to learn about it. <li id="cite_note-Vidmar327-79"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Vidmar327_79-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Vidmar, pp. 327–31</span>

<li id="cite_note-Pham45-80"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Pham45_80-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Pham, p. 45, quote: "When Pius XI was complimented on the publication, in 1937, of his encyclical denouncing Nazism, <i>Mit brennender Sorge</i>, his response was to point to his Secretary of State and say bluntly, 'The credit is his.<span style="padding-right:.15em;">'</span>"</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Bokenkotter389-81"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Bokenkotter389_81-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Bokenkotter, pp. 389–92, quote "And when Hitler showed increasing belligerance toward the Church, Pius met the challenge with a decisiveness that astonished the world. His encyclical <i>Mit brennender Sorge</i> was the 'first great official public document to dare to confront and criticize Nazism' and 'one of the greatest such condemnations ever issued by the Vatican'. Smuggled into Germany, it was read from all the Catholic pulpits on Palm Sunday in March 1937. It exposed the fallacy and denounced the Nazi myth of blood and soil; it decried its neopaganism, its war of annihilation against the Church, and even described the Führer himself as a 'mad prophet possessed of repulsive arrogance'. The Nazis were infuriated, and in retaliation closed and sealed all the presses that had printed it and took numerous vindictive measures against the Church, including staging a long series of immorality trials of the Catholic clergy."</span>

<li id="cite_note-306"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-306">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">"Lapide was in the 1960s an Israeli consul in Milan and was attempting, at the time he made his inflated estimates, to secure Vatican recognition for the state of Israel. Similar motives explain statements made in the immediate postwar period by Golda Meir and Moshe Sharett, foreign ministers of the new state of Israel. Had these statements been accurate within even an order of magnitude, Pius would perhaps deserve to be honored by <a href="/wiki/Yad_Vashem" title="Yad Vashem">Yad Vashem</a> and celebrated by Rabbi Dalin as a righteous gentile. They were not. Whatever was thus gained diplomatically by these statements — in the short run, precious little — was purchased at the cost of considerable historical untruth." in <i>Christian Century</i>, "Judging Pius XII" by Kevin Madigan. 14 March 2001, pp. 6–7; see also <i>The Pius war: responses to the critics of Pius XII</i> by <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Bottum_(author)" title="Joseph Bottum (author)">Joseph Bottum</a> &amp; David Dalin, 2004, p. 190</span>

<li id="cite_note-329"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-329">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">"brief phases of reassurance about the role of the Pope were followed by waves of critical literature[-] and counteracted the process of exoneration that had been underway for some years. The focus of recent analyses by John Cornwell via Michael Phayer, Susan Zucotti, Daniel J. Goldhagen, and Giovanni Miccoli, as well as works by authors Matteo Napolitano and Andrea Torniello, is once again about the Pope's silence about the murder of Jews in Europe -the papal archives could provide information about Vatican diplomacy between 1933 and 1945; however, the Vatican remains the only European state that withholds free access to its archives from contemporary historians. The archives of these years are crucial if many questions about the Holocaust and the Second World War are to be answered and if the many uncertainties concerning Nazi refugee assistance by the Vatican are to be removed." (Gerald Steinacher:Nazis on the Run, p. 105)</span>
